# Claim scope, corrections, and supersession

This register is a reading aid for the preserved historical files. It does not
rewrite their provenance. A historical “next question” may have been answered
later; a stored program's success message is not evidence for a theorem beyond
what it checks. Result statuses and proof paths are in
[the catalogue](../catalog/results.json).

## 1. Mathematical corrections that must remain adjacent in interpretation

| Claim to avoid | Correct statement and reason | Current source |

Their witness manifolds may differ. | [Foundations](../mathematics/current/skill/research/20260910.tex); [working map](../mathematics/current/skill/references/research-map.md) |

Ricci-flat metrics have \(\mathcal E=4\); positive actual monopole scalar energy excludes them separately in the applications. | [Main paper, Section 8](../mathematics/current/line1_integral_rigidity_paper.md) |

## 2. Scope distinctions that prevent false global conclusions

- **Local, neighborhood and all-output statements are different.** A
  conformal-class exclusion, a shrinking \(C^2\) repair ball, or a selected
  degeneration obstruction does not exclude distant Einstein metrics. The
  [all-mode repair theorem](../mathematics/current/round14_repair_radius.md)
  retains its shrinking radius; the [volume-component result](../mathematics/current/round13_global.md)
  leaves infinitely many components with accumulating volumes as a possibility.
- **No local Killing fields does not give a positive perturbation distance.**
  Arbitrarily small nonsymmetric perturbations may destroy every continuous
  local symmetry. [Round 12 symmetry](../mathematics/current/round12_symmetry.md)
  is an all-output structural restriction, not a no-repair theorem.
- **A realizable curvature orbit is not a forbidden model.** Hyperbolic
  products attain the product orbit and the KE root. More accurate supply of
  their auxiliary forms need not create a contradiction. The rank-one Weyl
  model is not a classification of every Einstein Weyl tensor.
- **Average structure is not exact structure.** Small \(\fint|\nabla\omega|^2\),
  harmonic alignment, or curvature-model distance alone does not create a
  global parallel form, complex structure or exact Einstein metric.
  The new [gauge realization theorem](../continuation/20260923_gauge_saturation_ke.md)
  does prove this implication with its actual gauge supply, topology and
  diameter hypotheses, via topology-preserving compactness. Its Section 7.2
  gives a real counterexample if diameter is deleted.
- **Scalar and Ricci nonattainment do not settle full-curvature attainment.**
  On the specified \(E_D\) and shear objects, the last is equivalent to
  Einstein existence; the first two are already established separately.
- **The product-boundary results have a volume-endpoint hypothesis.**
  [Rounds 15–16](../mathematics/current/round16.md) do not describe arbitrary
  exact metrics at other volumes. The later diameter-only theorem replaces
  those hypotheses only in its different, explicitly spectrally pinched class.
- **Complete noncompact models are not closed counterexamples.**
  Double cylinders, free-circle conformal ends, flat-torus ends, one-ended
  capped spaces, and fixed closed product-block assemblies are different
  topological and asymptotic problems.
- **Actual marking and smooth type matter.** \(A_1\) chirality freedom does
  not automatically extend to higher ADE cones. A homeomorphic KE surface
  does not supply a metric on the chosen standard smooth resolution. See
  [Round 19](../mathematics/current/round19_positive_bridge.md) and
  [Round 20](../mathematics/current/round20_global.md).
- **Finite spectra do not settle full matching.** The old multi-node
  coefficients retain truncation/tail errors; same-order self, deformation,
  inter-node and complement terms belong in the same obstruction equation.
- **No general flow shortcut has been proved.** Failure of the chosen Ricci
  flow to converge is not an all-metrics obstruction. The old common-time
  flow interface is not established merely by fixed-order estimates.

## 3. Superseded tasks and results that must not be rediscovered

| Historical wording or weaker stage | Present state |
| --- | --- |
| The specified blowup near-Einstein gap still needs a collapsed local-chart theorem. | The intrinsic Round-4 integral proof supplies this application by another mechanism. The broader chart/flow question remains separate. |
| \(b_+=1\), metricwise weights and Ricci-flat are open closure tasks. | The polarized proof, closure note and independent main manuscript contain these arguments under their precise gauge/chamber assumptions. |
| Exact two-ended free-torus completion is wholly open. | Round 9 excludes the exact smoothly conformally compact boundary-free-circle class. Nonsymmetric, nonfree and approximate variants are different questions. |
| Round 17 first removed constant scalar curvature from the integral gap. | Round 4 already allowed it. Round 17 generalized the different, realizable product-structure estimate. |
| Every old \(E_D\) correction restriction is still necessary. | The Round-6 theorem allows all tensor perturbations in its stated linear-size neighborhood; it is still a neighborhood theorem. |
| Removing exact Teichmüller kernels solves product degeneration. | Full Teichmüller-valued scalar channels remain, and many small positive modes may occur. Finite-monodromy local realization is proved only with constants for each fixed geometry. |
| \(\#_4\mathbb{CP}^2\) is a new positive candidate. | The old strict Hitchin–Thorpe Ricci-box obstruction already excludes arbitrarily tight pinching of either sign; Round 19 is a strengthened endpoint check/recall, not a new candidate. |
| More unknown ordinary monopoles may push the stated hyperbolic surgery sequence to ratio \(2/3\). | Round 19 tests all eligible projection budgets and gives the asymptotic ceiling corresponding to ratio at least \(3/4\). |
| Noncommuting free-quotient monodromy is a new construction target. | Round 11 already permits arbitrary free-quotient monodromy. Round 20 instead isolates a faithful surface relation not factoring through a free group. |
| An opposite-Weyl bound is still required for the latest actual shear spectral gap. | The aspherical no-bubbling proof removes it for the specified self-gluings. The older broader bounded-geometry theorem remains valid in its own setting. |
| Relative pointwise pinching is still required for that exact shear spectral gap. | [Spectral compactness](../continuation/20260923_allowed_mode_rigidity.md), Theorem 4.1 and Corollary 8.1, now remove it. Diameter remains. |
| Only aspherical smooth topology can use the compact-core argument. | The intermediate topology extension used torsion-free \(\pi_1\) and \(\pi_2=0\); the [latest all-ALE proof](../continuation/20260924_ale_boundary_compatibility.md) needs only \(\pi_2=0\), by a finite-cover lift. |
| The latest realization always needs a Weyl bound, monopole square cost, or gauge saturation. | The latest all-ALE proof needs none for general Einstein existence with \(\pi_2=0\), diameter and positive volume. Separate actual-form hypotheses still identify stronger KE geometry. |
| A finite-range \(c_L\) can be made uniform over unbounded \(\beta\) without decay. | KE models forbid that. The compactified theorem includes the explicit factor \((\beta-2)^2/\beta^4\). |

## 4. Attribution, novelty and what verification means

### 24 September: stronger realization inputs and corrected output logic

- **A KE output completes Einstein existence on the covered input class.**
  It is not an additional output limitation. Uncovered input classes remain
  the issue; this must not be confused with nonexistence, for which excluding
  KE alone would be insufficient.
- **The mixed-chirality ALE rational-ball alternative is no longer open under
  vanishing second homotopy.** The earlier notes correctly stopped at what
  their half-flat classification supplied. The stronger existing
  Zhang/Bamler–Chen nonembedding lemma and a finite-cover lift now exclude
  every nonflat Ricci-flat ALE core; see
  [the new proof](../continuation/20260924_ale_boundary_compatibility.md).
  Neither a torsion-free fundamental group nor a vanishing Weyl half is
  needed. The older proof is preserved, not declared false.
- **Saturation is no longer needed for general Einstein existence** in that
  compact, noncollapsed, \(\pi_2=0\) regime. It still provides stronger
  KE geometry in the separate actual-form argument. The
  [bilateral theorem](../continuation/20260924_bilateral_realization.md)
  and [Chern-boundary theorem](../continuation/20260924_chern_boundary_realization.md)
  instead remove homotopy assumptions by controlling both curvature halves.
- **Diameter-free repair has different hypotheses, not none.** The
  [local-to-global theorem](../continuation/20260924_local_to_global_scope.md)
  retains unit-ball noncollapse, a full tensor linearized gap, Ricci regularity
  and small unnormalized residual. These do not follow from pointwise Ricci
  smallness by definition.

### Further 24 September scope updates

- Gauge supply on a finite cover need not descend as a class or form.
  [I28](../continuation/20260924_virtual_gauge_realization.md) descends its
  curvature invariants and constructs the exact metric on the base. An
  upstairs existence theorem alone would not suffice. Global Kahler output
  is deliberately not asserted, with a non-almost-complex quotient test.
- The unnormalized residual in I27 is no longer required under the
  **additional growth and positive-coercivity assumptions** of
  [I29](../continuation/20260924_uniformly_local_repair.md). An absolute
  spectral gap alone is not this positive coercivity condition.
- [I30](../continuation/20260924_collapsed_bilateral_bulk.md) controls local
  rewound geometry on a maximal-rank bulk. Relative small bad-set volume
  neither removes its topology nor supplies the global Einstein inverse.
- [I31](../continuation/20260924_orbifold_smoothing_bridge.md) removes
  \(\pi_2=0\) for orbifold compactification, **not** for unconditional
  same-smooth-type realization. Global QG smoothing and whole-filling
  boundary-marked Milnor matching remain explicit additional conditions.

The project distinguishes a new local deduction from a new theorem in the
literature. Bauer–Furuta nonvanishing, symplectic geography, cscK blowups,
Derdziński classification, Gursky–LeBrun gaps, Einstein compactness, ALE
classification and causal boundary noncommunication remain credited external
inputs. Applying them to an actual object with verified hypotheses can be a
substantive project result; it does not rediscover the source theorem.

The parameter-family nonnegativity and classical envelope endpoints are
algebraic consequences of established curvature inequalities. The claimed
increment is their quantitative non-saturation under approximate Einstein
input and its realized applications. The catalogue records this distinction.

Reproduction programs certify their named finite algebra, coordinate Ricci
identities, arithmetic, or numerical samples. Compilation establishes readable
artifacts. Neither is a replacement for PDE, smooth-topology, gauge-theory or
global existence proofs. Historical evaluation phrases such as “major
breakthrough,” “four-journal candidate,” or “complete Skill” are not mathematical
dependencies and are not promoted to result status.

### 26 September: backbone, extensions and research choices

The [current guidance](../guidance/LINE1_CURRENT.md) records an organizational
correction, not a withdrawal of proved scope. The integral obstruction,
polarized applications, compactified functional pencil, two-sided energy
window and actual BF/twisted applications remain the first-line backbone.
Completed restricted realization is a positive extension. The proposed
fixed-radius all-mode classification, intrinsic global transition recovery,
and whole-core symplectic extension are not yet theorem-catalogue entries.

“Prioritize whole-core filling” applies to enlarging existence scope; it does
not make filling a prerequisite for the obstruction paper. Existing
period-inertia and unbounded-pencil results must not become fresh to-dos.
Older charter status is updated by I24–I37, not used to restore hypotheses
already removed in those manuscripts.

The [prior analysis](../guidance/source/20260926_assistant_priorities.zh.md)
also records a scale calibration: small global rescaling of a degenerating
negative KE family preserves pointwise approximation and zero cscK deficit
but can violate the unmodified total-defect/core-scale smallness ratio.
This does not refute I37's sufficient condition or settle a scale-optimized
replacement. It changes which automatic implication is worth pursuing; it
is not registered here as a newly established research theorem.

## 原稿 2: round19_global_gap.md

来源：`mathematics/current/round19_global_gap.md`；范围：`full`。

原文件 SHA-256：`f81e680c7e180f440074eaad6f94fe7b15292eb5cf125d733aba28f24ee52fa7`。

# Hyperbolic surgery: quantitative comparison and the monopole bridge

22 September 2026. This note concerns smooth closed oriented four-manifolds,
not the local Einstein repair problem. Its new compatibility conclusion is
that fixed-piece hyperbolic dilution families stay asymptotically on the
`T / A >= 3/4` side for every positive uniform actual-monopole projection
budget `A`, even with metric-dependent choice among all unknown ordinary
monopole classes. Thus their all-sign nonexistence does not connect to the
actual-monopole integral theorem near `2/3`. Sections 1--3 give the complete
comparison inequality, including its Weyl and volume-stability parameters.
Section 4 resolves a dangerous quantifier in a possible stability shortcut.
No topology-independent pointwise Ricci gap is asserted.

## 1. The exact Ricci-box calculation

Let `f:M -> (N,h)` have nonzero degree `D`, where `h` has sectional curvature
`-1`. Put

\[
 H=|D|\chi(N),\quad V_H=|D|\operatorname{Vol}(N,h)
       ={4\pi^2\over3}H,\quad a={\chi(M)\over H}.
\]

Suppose `0 <= delta < 1` and every Ricci eigenvalue is `-3 x_i`, with
`1-delta <= x_i <= 1+delta`. Write `E0=Ric-(s/4)g` and use the
endomorphism norm on the Weyl operator. The Ricci term in Chern--Gauss--Bonnet
is

\[
 Q={s^2\over24}-{ |E_0|^2\over2}
   ={3\over2}\left((\textstyle\sum_i x_i)^2
                                    -3\sum_i x_i^2\right).
 \tag{1}
\]

It is separately concave, so its minimum on the cube occurs at a vertex.
At a vertex with `m` upper endpoints its value is

\[
 Q_m=6\{1+(m-2)\delta+[(m-2)^2-3]\delta^2\}.
 \tag{2}
\]

Comparison of these five expressions gives the exact box minimum

\[
 {Q_{\min}(\delta)\over6}=
 \begin{cases}
 (1-\delta)^2,&0\le\delta\le1/3,\\
 (1-2\delta)(1+\delta),&1/3\le\delta\le1.
 \end{cases}                                                   \tag{3}
\]

The first branch is attained at four lower endpoints; the second at one
upper and three lower endpoints. This is an algebraically optimal bound
from the Ricci interval alone, not an existence statement for a metric
simultaneously saturating the global comparisons.

BCG's degree inequality and Bishop comparison give

\[
 h(g)^4V\ge81V_H,\qquad h(g)\le3\sqrt{1+\delta},\qquad
 \rho:={(1+\delta)^2V\over V_H}\ge1.
 \tag{4}
\]

The input is the main theorem and Ricci-volume corollary on printed p.734
of [Besson--Courtois--Gallot (1995)](https://www.aimath.org/WWN/entropyrigidity/papers/BessonCourtoisGallot.pdf).
Define the scale-invariant normalized Weyl energy

\[
 \omega={1\over8\pi^2H}\int_M|W|^2.
\]

Chern--Gauss--Bonnet now implies the joint inequality

\[
 \boxed{\quad a\ge\omega+\rho\,b(\delta),\qquad
 b(\delta)=
 \begin{cases}
 [(1-\delta)/(1+\delta)]^2,&0\le\delta\le1/3,\\
 (1-2\delta)/(1+\delta),&1/3\le\delta<1/2.
 \end{cases}\quad}                                           \tag{5}
\]

We use positivity of `b` only for `delta<1/2`. For larger delta, multiplying
a volume lower bound by a negative box minimum reverses its usefulness;
there is no extension of the resulting gap by that argument.

For `0 <= z <= 1` define

\[
 G(z)=
 \begin{cases}
 (1-z)/(2+z),&0\le z\le1/4,\\
 (1-\sqrt z)/(1+\sqrt z),&1/4\le z\le1.
 \end{cases}                                                 \tag{6}
\]

This is the exact inverse of `b` on `[0,1/2]`. If an independent theorem
gives `rho>=1+gamma` and `a-omega` lies in `[0,1+gamma]`, then

\[
 \boxed{\quad \delta\ge
 G\!\left({a-\omega\over1+\gamma}\right).\quad}             \tag{7}
\]

For `delta>=1/2` the bound is automatic. If `a-omega<0`, no metric with
`delta<1/2` satisfies the assumptions. Formula (7) optimizes the algebraic
consequence of the specified three scalar inputs; it does not assert a
sharp geometric infimum.

## 2. What the topology-only inequality does and does not say

For `M_j=N_j#(S^1 x S^3)`, put `H_j=chi(N_j)`. The degree-one collapse
map gives `a_j=1-2/H_j`. For `M=N#N`, its degree-two fold gives
`H=2 chi(N)` and again `a=1-2/H`. The topology-only conclusion, when
`a>=1/4`, is

\[
 \delta\ge {1-\sqrt{1-2/H}\over1+\sqrt{1-2/H}}
          ={1\over2H}+O(H^{-2}).                            \tag{8}
\]

The `N#N` specialization is the inherited `1/(4 chi(N))` asymptotic.
At the algebraic endpoint `chi(M)=0`, the second branch gives the
elementary conclusion `delta>=1/2`.
More generally a fixed number `k` of Euler-loss-two surgeries replaces
`2/H` by `2k/H` wherever such a degree comparison is available.

Nonnegative Ricci curvature is already excluded by (4), since it gives
zero entropy on the universal cover. In particular every nonnegative
Einstein constant is excluded, and positive-normalized relative Ricci
defect cannot be less than one. At negative Einstein normalization,
`delta=0`, (5) contradicts `a<1`. These are all-metrics, all-Einstein-sign
obstructions.

Equality rigidity alone does not replace (8) by a positive constant
independent of `H`. Indeed equality in its derivation would require
simultaneous equality in BCG, the Ricci-box bound, and `W=0`. BCG forces
the Ricci-lower-bound normalization to be hyperbolic, which does not
saturate the opposite corner in (3) when `delta>0`. Thus an individual
metric does not attain the positive threshold. Nonattainment gives no
positive distance of an infimum from that threshold.

There is a useful falsifiable consequence for any hypothetical sequence
with `H_j -> infinity`, `a_j -> 1`, and `delta_j -> 0`:

\[
 1\le\rho_j\le {a_j\over b(\delta_j)}\longrightarrow1,
 \qquad
 0\le\omega_j\le a_j-b(\delta_j)\longrightarrow0.            \tag{9}
\]

The second statement is `integral |W|^2 / H_j -> 0`, not absolute Weyl
energy tending to zero. Even a positive absolute energy cost for each
fixed surgery is compatible with (9). To improve (8) uniformly by this
route requires an extensive estimate such as `omega>=w0>0` or
`rho>=1+gamma0>1`, uniform over the changing topology. A single energy
quantum gives another term of order `1/H_j`.

## 3. The legitimate fixed-target stability improvement

[Bessières--Besson--Courtois--Gallot, Duke 161 (2012)](https://www.math.u-bordeaux.fr/~labessie/bbcg_duke.pdf),
Theorem 1.3, gives a positive relative volume gap for a degree-one map to
a fixed closed hyperbolic target, unless that map is homotopic to a
diffeomorphism. Its constant depends on dimension and target diameter;
no source noncollapse or source diameter bound is an extra assumption.

For the degree-one map `N_j#(S^1 x S^3) -> N_j`, rescale to
`g'=(1+delta)g`. Then `Ric(g')>=-3g'`. The map cannot be homotopic to a
diffeomorphism, already because Euler characteristics differ. Thus

\[
 \rho>1+\gamma(4,\operatorname{diam}N_j),
 \quad
 \delta\ge G\left({1-2/H_j\over
                         1+\gamma(4,\operatorname{diam}N_j)}\right).
 \tag{10}
\]

For one fixed target, this improves the elementary comparison with a
nonexplicit positive amount. For `N#N`, their fixed-target Corollary 1.7
similarly supplies a relative volume gap above `2 Vol(N)` and hence the
same formula with `H=2 chi(N)` and a constant depending on `N`.
This uses the separately stated fixed-degree conclusion of Corollary 1.7;
the failure of a constant uniform in unbounded mapping degree, discussed
next, does not refute that fixed-degree assertion.

The target diameters of unbounded-degree hyperbolic covers cannot remain
bounded: their volumes diverge whereas a hyperbolic ball of bounded radius
has bounded volume. Formula (10) therefore supplies no common positive
constant for this cover family. That is a failure of the available
estimate, not a proof that the actual Ricci infima tend to zero.

## 4. Why composition to a fixed base cannot silently remove the dependence

The printed Theorem 1.6 in the same paper, p.32, states an arbitrary-degree
version with a constant depending only on dimension and target diameter;
its proof is omitted. Taken literally, it would apply after composing
`M_j -> N_j -> N`, and would imply a uniform gap in (10) depending only
on the fixed base `N`. This tempting inference is not used here. Its
uniform-in-degree quantifier conflicts with the following independent
test in even higher dimension. This is a mathematical inconsistency in
that literal reading, not a claim about a published erratum.

Fix a closed real-hyperbolic eight-manifold `X` and an exotic eight-sphere
`Sigma`. The Farrell--Jones construction, as stated by Farrell in
[Farrell--Ontaneda, Theorem 1](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0402173), gives,
for `eta_j -> 0`, finite covers `X_j -> X` and metrics `k_j` on
`Y_j=X_j#Sigma` with sectional curvatures in `(-1-eta_j,-1+eta_j)`,
although `Y_j` and `X_j` are not diffeomorphic. The original paper is
Farrell--Jones, JAMS 2 (1989), 899--908, DOI
`10.1090/S0894-0347-1989-1002632-2`; the publisher PDF could not be fetched
in this run. The linked primary-author account supplies precisely the
fixed-base, finite-cover, pinching statement used below.

Here is direct volume control, avoiding an unverified sharp simplicial
volume estimate. Sectional evaluation is an injective linear map on the
finite-dimensional space of algebraic curvature tensors. Norm equivalence
therefore gives

\[
 |\operatorname{Rm}_{k_j}-\operatorname{Rm}_{-1}|\le C_8\eta_j.
\]

The normalized Euler density is a degree-four polynomial of curvature;
at constant curvature `-1` it is `c_8=2/Vol(S^8)>0`. Consequently

\[
 |e(k_j)-c_8|\le C'_8\eta_j.
\]

Since `chi(Y_j)=chi(X_j)=c_8 Vol(X_j)`, Gauss--Bonnet gives, for a
dimension-dependent `C` and small `eta_j`,

\[
 {1\over1+C\eta_j}\le
 {\operatorname{Vol}(Y_j,k_j)\over\operatorname{Vol}(X_j)}
 \le {1\over1-C\eta_j}.                                    \tag{11}
\]

Set `k'_j=(1+eta_j)k_j`; then `Ric(k'_j)>=-7k'_j`, and the volume ratio
still tends to one because volume rescales by `(1+eta_j)^4`. Composing
the connected-sum homeomorphism `Y_j -> X_j` with the cover to `X`
gives degree `d_j` and

\[
 {\operatorname{Vol}(Y_j,k'_j)\over d_j\operatorname{Vol}(X)}
 \longrightarrow1.                                        \tag{12}
\]

If this composite map were homotopic to a smooth covering, its induced
subgroup determines the cover `X_j`; uniqueness of the smooth lifted
structure would make `Y_j` diffeomorphic to `X_j`, a contradiction.
Thus a dimension-and-fixed-base-only version of Theorem 1.6 cannot hold
uniformly in mapping degree as printed. A corrected dependence or a
separate four-dimensional proof is required before using that shortcut.
Nothing in this test constructs a four-dimensional Ricci approximation
or excludes all Einstein metrics on the eight-dimensional examples.

## 5. A quantitative all-monopole separation for the dilution family

Use the actual fixed-piece construction of
[round18_integral_limits.md](round18_integral_limits.md):
`M_j=N_j#(S^1 x S^3)` agrees with the hyperbolic metric outside one fixed
deleted ball `B`, and a single fixed metric `k` on the replacement `K`
supplies the whole modification. Put

\[
 H_j=\chi(N_j),\quad T_j=2\chi(M_j)+3\tau(M_j)=2H_j-4,
 \quad q=-s+\sqrt6|W^+|.
\]

Hyperbolic four-manifolds have signature zero. A connected sum with
`S^1 x S^3` changes neither the intersection form nor its signature.
On the exterior `s=-12`, `W^+=0`, and `q=12`. Therefore

\[
 \int_{M_j}q^2=144\operatorname{Vol}(N_j)+C_q,
 \quad C_q=\int_K(-s_k+\sqrt6|W_k^+|)^2-144\operatorname{Vol}(B),
 \tag{13}
\]

where `C_q` is independent of `j`; it need not be positive. For all
sufficiently large `j`, `b^+(M_j)>=2`, since `tau=0` and
`b_2=H_j-2+2b_1(N_j) -> infinity`.

Let `C(M_j)` be the finite set of all real ordinary monopole classes;
when it is empty, take its maximum projection norm to be zero.
Distinguish the invariant from [round5_rigidity.md](round5_rigidity.md)
from the infimum restricted to actual metric period planes:

\[
 \alpha_{\rm all}^2(M_j)=
 \inf_{P\in\operatorname{Gr}_{b^+}^{+}}
                 \max_{c\in C(M_j)}(c_P^+)^2,
 \qquad
 A_{\rm met}(M_j)=\inf_{g\text{ smooth}}
                 \max_{c\in C(M_j)}(c_g^+)^2.
\]

Every metric period plane is a maximal positive plane, so
`alpha_all^2 <= A_met`. We do not identify these two infima or assume
surjectivity of the metric period map. A number `A_j>0` is a uniform
actual-monopole projection budget if every smooth metric admits some
actual class `c_g` with `(c_g^+)^2>=A_j`. Such a choice may depend on the
metric and need not be continuous. Its defining property directly gives

\[
 A_j\le\inf_g\max_{c\in C(M_j)}(c_g^+)^2=A_{\rm met}(M_j).
 \tag{13a}
\]

The classical conformal-monopole inequality
`72 pi^2 (c_g^+)^2 <= integral q^2` applies to each class for this same
metric; see [LeBrun, Theorem 2.3](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0003068).
The square, without positive-part truncation, suffices here and requires
no sign condition on the curvature of `K`. Hence (13) gives

\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \alpha_{\rm all}^2(M_j)\le A_{\rm met}(M_j)
                    \le {8\over3}H_j+c_q,
 \qquad c_q={C_q\over72\pi^2}.
 \quad}                                                     \tag{14}
\]

The same fixed-piece test metric bounds the maximum over all classes,
and hence bounds `A_met` itself, not merely the infimum over all abstract
positive planes. Together with (13a), this covers unknown classes and
arbitrary metric dependence of their selection. It assumes
neither a uniform negative square bound for classes nor an Einstein
metric on `M_j`. If `A_met=0`, there is no positive uniform projection
budget to feed into the integral theorem. Vanishing of `alpha_all^2`
alone would not justify that conclusion.

For every positive uniform budget `A_j`, including `A_met` itself when
positive, sufficiently large `j` therefore satisfies

\[
 \boxed{\quad
 {T_j\over A_j}
 \ge {2H_j-4\over (8/3)H_j+c_q},\qquad
 \liminf_j{T_j\over A_j}\ge{3\over4}.
 \quad}                                                     \tag{15}
\]

With `c_q^+=max(c_q,0)`, the explicit sufficient threshold

\[
 H_j\ge36+{51\over8}c_q^+
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 {T_j\over A_j}\ge{17\over24}
              ={2\over3}+{1\over24}                         \tag{16}
\]

follows by multiplying out the positive denominator. For completeness,
if `v=sum t_i c_i` is a convex combination of classes and `P` is positive,
then `v^2 <= (v_P^+)^2 <= sum t_i(c_{i,P}^+)^2 <= max_i(c_{i,P}^+)^2`.
Thus `beta_all^2<=alpha_all^2<=A_met`, while restricting to special
classes gives `beta_sp^2<=alpha_sp^2<=alpha_all^2<=A_met`. The same
separation applies to all these invariants when positive. This is stronger than merely
observing that the current integral theorem fails to prove a gap on the
family: the numerical invariant itself stays away from its critical band.

The proof also applies to a fixed number `k` of these connected-sum
operations, or to fixed-number connected sums of growing covers joined
by fixed metric connectors. Let `H_j` be the sum of hyperbolic Euler
characteristics and suppose the resulting topology has
`tau=0`, `chi=H_j-2k`. The metric modification contributes a fixed constant
to (13), so

\[
 {T_j\over A_j}
 \ge {2H_j-4k\over(8/3)H_j+c_q},\qquad
 H_j\ge36k+{51\over8}c_q^+
 \ \Longrightarrow\ {T_j\over A_j}\ge17/24.
 \tag{17}
\]

Reversing orientation changes the fixed constant to the one computed
with `W^-`, but not the `3/4` limit or the form of the explicit threshold.
These metrics have a fixed, nonzero maximum Ricci defect. Equations
(14)--(17) are not a construction of decreasing maximum defect. They
show why replacing the missing pointwise estimate by the existing
near-`2/3` actual-monopole argument does not settle these surgery types.

## 6. Precisely what remains testable

A pointwise counterexample from the growing-cover surgery family would
have to provide actual metrics with `delta_j -> 0` and satisfy (9).
A proof of a uniform pointwise obstruction would need an additional
uniform estimate: for example an extensive volume excess, extensive
Weyl cost, or a local surgery obstruction that controls the maximum
defect independently of the ambient hyperbolic volume. Neither the
Ricci-box algebra, an absolute energy quantum, nor equality
nonattainment supplies such an estimate. The known fixed-piece metrics
test the integral quantifier, while (14) tests the monopole-bridge
quantifier; neither decides the outstanding pointwise construction.

`round19_global_gap_check.py` checks the box polynomials, their exact
minimum branches, inversion of (5), and the explicit all-monopole-band
threshold. These are algebraic verifications, not computer proofs of
BCG, stability, or monopole existence.

## 原稿 3: line1_rank_one_entropy_scope.md

来源：`mathematics/current/line1_rank_one_entropy_scope.md`；范围：`full`。

原文件 SHA-256：`b9b99c0d814f2fe6d330faec1774c2620887ccdfe4ea396d9a5d529425ac375d`。

# Rank-one degree entropy does not add a budget in the classical monopole family

23 September 2026. This is a composition of classical mapping rigidity,
canonical-divisor positivity, and curvature inequalities. It closes one
specific proposed route to an independent budget; it is not a new Einstein
nonexistence theorem. It applies to the entire classical family below, not
only to the ball-quotient examples used in the two-chirality application.

## 1. Statement

Let
\[
 M=X\#k\overline{\mathbb {CP}}^{\,2}\#m(S^1\times S^3),\qquad
 A=c_1^2(X)>0,\quad h=(k+4m)/A,
\]
where \(X\) is a closed minimal complex surface of general type and
\(k,m\ge0\). Give \(X\) its complex orientation. Then:

1. Every continuous map from \(M\) to a closed real-hyperbolic
   four-manifold has degree zero.
2. If \(f:M\to Y\) maps to a closed complex-hyperbolic surface and
   \(D=|\deg f|>0\), then
   \[
   \boxed{D\,c_1^2(Y)\le A.}                              \tag{1}
   \]
3. For every metric whose Ricci eigenvalues lie in
   \([-1-\delta,-1+\delta]\), with \(0\le\delta<1\), the classical
   scalar monopole bound gives
   \[
   \boxed{\operatorname{Vol}(M,g)
          \ge\frac{2\pi^2 A}{(1+\delta)^2}.}              \tag{2}
   \]
   Every ordinary degree-to-complex-hyperbolic BCG/Bishop lower bound is
   no larger than \(64/81\) times the right side of (2).

Consequently, the ordinary rank-one target degree inequalities do not
strengthen the available volume lower budget for pointwise negative
Ricci input on this family. For exact negative Einstein candidates their
energy and Chern--Weil consequences are already weaker than the classical
monopole Ricci-energy bound. This is a comparison of these specified
inequalities, not a claim that all entropy, representation, or harmonic-map
methods have been exhausted.

## 2. Why all degree comes from the Kähler summand

For any aspherical target \(Y\), pinch the connected-sum necks to obtain
\[
 q:M\longrightarrow
 X\vee\bigvee_{1}^{k}\overline{\mathbb {CP}}^{\,2}
      \vee\bigvee_{1}^{m}(S^1\times S^3).
\]
This map induces the free-product isomorphism on fundamental groups.
Maps to \(Y=K(\pi_1Y,1)\) are classified, up to free homotopy, by
fundamental-group homomorphisms up to conjugation. Thus every \(f\) is
homotopic to \(F\circ q\) for a map on the wedge, obtained by restricting
its fundamental-group homomorphism to the summands. The fundamental class
is sent by \(q\) to the sum of their fundamental classes, so degrees add.

The simply connected projective-plane summands map null-homotopically.
A map from \(S^1\times S^3\) to \(Y\) is homotopic to one factoring
through \(S^1\), since this realizes the same fundamental-group map.
These summands therefore have degree zero. There is a continuous map
\(f_X:X\to Y\) satisfying
\[
 \deg f_X=\deg f.                                        \tag{3}
\]
This argument does not presume that the unknown metric on \(M\) is
Kähler or that an embedded copy of the punctured \(X\) is geometrically
adapted to it.

Minimal surfaces of general type are projective and hence Kähler. Give
\(X\) any Kähler metric and replace \(f_X\) by its harmonic representative
into the closed nonpositively curved target. For a real-hyperbolic target,
Sampson's rank theorem gives rank at most two, so its four-dimensional
degree is zero. For a complex-hyperbolic target, a nonzero degree forces
real rank four somewhere; harmonic rigidity then makes the map
holomorphic or antiholomorphic. These precise alternatives are recorded
in Carlson--Toledo, Corollary 3.7; harmonic representative existence is
also used there. In complex dimension two conjugating the target complex
structure preserves its real orientation and its canonical square.

## 3. The degree bound is a canonical intersection calculation

We can therefore assume \(f_X:X\to Y\) is holomorphic of degree \(D\).
It is surjective and generically finite. The determinant of its
differential gives the canonical divisor identity
\[
 K_X=f_X^*K_Y+R,
\]
where the ramification divisor \(R\) is effective. The canonical class
\(K_X\) is nef because \(X\) is minimal of general type; \(K_Y\) is
ample, so \(f_X^*K_Y\) is nef. It follows that
\[
 A-Dc_1^2(Y)
 =K_X^2-(f_X^*K_Y)^2
 =R\cdot(K_X+f_X^*K_Y)\ge0.
\]
This proves (1), including maps with ramification and exceptional fibres.
No assumption that \(f_X\) is an unramified covering is used.

## 4. Compare the budgets at the actual pointwise normalization

Normalize the complex-hyperbolic metric \(g_Y\) by
\(\operatorname{Ric}_{g_Y}=-g_Y\), and write \(B=c_1^2(Y)\). Then
\[
 V_Y=2\pi^2B,\qquad h_{\rm vol}(g_Y)^4=64/9.
\]
For example, the holomorphic-sectional-curvature \(-4\) normalization
has Ricci \(-6g\) and entropy \(4\); scaling by six gives the displayed
values. Equivalently they give exactly the \(32/81\) coefficient in
Kotschick's complex-hyperbolic degree obstruction.

BCG's degree inequality and Bishop comparison under
\(\operatorname{Ric}_g\ge-(1+\delta)g\) give
\[
 h_{\rm vol}(g)^4 V\ge (64/9)D V_Y,
 \qquad h_{\rm vol}(g)^4\le9(1+\delta)^2.
\]
Hence the lower number furnished by this method is
\[
 \boxed{V\ge\frac{64}{81}
              \frac{2\pi^2DB}{(1+\delta)^2}.}             \tag{4}
\]
On the other hand, the polarized actual monopole class on \(M\) has
\((a_g^+)^2\ge A\), including the classical small-\(b_+\) chamber and
handle-insertion hypotheses supplied in the inherited closure proof.
The scalar inequality gives
\[
 32\pi^2A\le\int s^2
           \le16(1+\delta)^2V,
\]
which is (2). By (1), the right side of (4) is at most \(64/81\) of
the right side of (2). This comparison is uniform over every target
and every degree map. An arbitrary collection of such lower bounds
can be maximized, not added; its supremum remains dominated.

For any normalized exact negative Einstein metric put
\(e=\int|\operatorname{Ric}|^2/(8\pi^2A)=V/(2\pi^2A)\).
Equation (4) reads \(e\ge(64/81)DB/A\le64/81\).
The classical all-metrics Ricci bound is stronger:
\[
 e\ge1+h.                                                \tag{5}
\]
Indeed, the scalar--Weyl defect \(\mathscr D_{\rm LB}\ge0\) gives
\(\int|\operatorname{Ric}|^2
 =8\pi^2(2p-T)+32\pi^2\mathscr D_{\rm LB}\), with
\(p\ge A\), \(T=A(1-h)\). Thus (5) does not depend on attainment of an
energy infimum.

In the range \(0<h<1/3\), the stronger classical Einstein envelope
\(e\ge F(h)>1+h\) already implies all these entropy lower bounds.
In terms of the two-chirality upper budget \(U\), Kotschick's necessary
degree inequality is \(U>(64/81)DB/A\); any candidate with
\(U\ge F(h)\) automatically passes it. It therefore cannot close the
remaining region \(U-F(h)>0\) by this particular comparison.

## 5. Scope and research consequence

This note provides a family-wide reason to stop repeatedly seeking the
missing budget from ordinary four-dimensional rank-one target degree
comparisons on the classical family. The result includes the realized
ball-quotient cover examples, but is not limited to them.

It does not cover Bauer--Furuta connected sums of non-Kähler symplectic
summands: the Kähler source and nef canonical argument above would have
to be replaced. It also does not bound the manifold's actual minimal
entropy from above, rule out additional quantitative stability inputs,
or concern targets of higher rank, representations without ordinary
degree maps, noncompact targets, or orbifold degree transfers.
It supplies no new pointwise approximation and no new no-Einstein type.
The unrestricted integral dilution examples in Round 18 and the
real-hyperbolic surgery comparison in Round 19 remain different results.

## Sources and finite checks

- Carlson--Toledo, *Harmonic mappings of Kähler manifolds to locally
  symmetric spaces*, IHÉS 69 (1989), Corollaries 3.3--3.7:
  <https://www.numdam.org/item/10.1007/BF02698844.pdf>.
- Besson--Courtois--Gallot, *Entropies et rigidités des espaces localement
  symétriques de courbure strictement négative*, GAFA 5 (1995), main theorem:
  <https://www.aimath.org/WWN/entropyrigidity/papers/BessonCourtoisGallot.pdf>.
- Kotschick, *Entropies, volumes, and Einstein metrics*, Corollary 3,
  equation (15): <https://arxiv.org/html/math/0410215>.
- LeBrun, *Ricci Curvature, Minimal Volumes, and Seiberg--Witten Theory*,
  Proposition 3.2 and the Ricci-energy inequality:
  <https://arxiv.org/html/math/0003068>.

`check_line1_rank_one_entropy_scope.py` checks the scale factors, the
volume/energy domination, and the translation to Kotschick's coefficient.
It does not check the harmonic-map or Seiberg--Witten existence theorems.

## 原稿 4: round18_integral_limits.md

来源：`mathematics/current/round18_integral_limits.md`；范围：`full`。

原文件 SHA-256：`78878086ec31fe9756e6835a641bf56ae22781570ef8d3eea08273984c4fb797`。

# A complete test of the unrestricted integral extension

The actual-monopole and relative topology hypotheses of the integral
rigidity theorem cannot simply be discarded. The following classical
construction gives a concrete reason, even if collapse and curvature
blow-up are excluded. Its role is to distinguish a viable extension from
a false one, not to supply a pointwise Yau26 counterexample.

## 1. Statement

There are closed oriented smooth four-manifolds \(M_j\) and smooth
metrics \(g_j\) such that:

1. No \(M_j\) admits an Einstein metric of any sign.
2. For every nonnegative integer \(r\), there are constants \(C_r\),
   and there is \(i_0>0\), independent of \(j\), with
   \[
   |\nabla^r\operatorname{Rm}_{g_j}|\le C_r,\qquad
   \operatorname{inj}(M_j,g_j)\ge i_0 .
   \tag{1}
   \]
3. The volume tends to infinity and, for every \(1\le p<\infty\),
   \[
   \fint_{M_j}|\operatorname{Ric}_{g_j}+3g_j|^p
      =\frac{C_p}{\operatorname{Vol}(M_j,g_j)}\longrightarrow0 .
   \tag{2}
   \]
   In particular the relative integral Einstein defect satisfies
   \[
   \mathcal E(g_j):=
   \inf_{\lambda\ne0}
   \frac{\int|\operatorname{Ric}_{g_j}-\lambda g_j|^2}
        {\lambda^2\operatorname{Vol}(M_j,g_j)}
   \le\frac{C_2}{9\operatorname{Vol}(M_j,g_j)}\longrightarrow0 .
   \tag{3}
   \]
4. The pointwise operator-norm defect of these metrics is a fixed
   positive number:
   \[
   \|g_j^{-1}\operatorname{Ric}_{g_j}+3I\|_\infty=c_\infty>0.
   \tag{4}
   \]

Thus a dimension-only relative integral gap on all no-Einstein
four-manifolds is false, including within a uniformly bounded-geometry
class. This statement permits the smooth topology and total volume to
vary; it asserts neither \(\inf_g\mathcal E(g)=0\) on one fixed \(M_j\)
nor pointwise Einstein approximation.

## 2. The manifolds and the all-sign obstruction

Fix a closed connected oriented real-hyperbolic four-manifold
\((N,h)\), with sectional curvature \(-1\). Its finitely generated
linear fundamental group is infinite and residually finite. It therefore
has connected finite covers \(N_j\to N\) of unbounded degree \(d_j\).
For example, the intersections of finitely many finite-index subgroups
separating successive nontrivial elements form a tower of unbounded
index. Write \(h_j\) for the lifted hyperbolic metric and set
\[
 M_j=N_j\#(S^1\times S^3),\qquad
 V_N=\operatorname{Vol}(N,h).
 \tag{5}
\]
Then
\[
 \chi(M_j)=d_j\chi(N)-2,\qquad
 \operatorname{Vol}(N_j,h_j)=d_jV_N
                  =\frac{4\pi^2}{3}d_j\chi(N).
 \tag{6}
\]
Collapsing the second punctured summand gives a degree-one map
\(M_j\to N_j\).

The Besson--Courtois--Gallot degree inequality gives
\[
 h_{\mathrm{vol}}(g)^4\operatorname{Vol}(M_j,g)
                         \ge3^4d_jV_N .
 \tag{7}
\]
If \(g\) were Einstein with nonnegative Einstein constant, Bishop
comparison on its universal cover would give zero volume entropy,
contradicting (7).

If the constant is negative, rescale to \(\operatorname{Ric}_g=-3g\).
The sharp real-hyperbolic Ricci-volume consequence of (7) gives
\(\operatorname{Vol}(M_j,g)\ge d_jV_N\).
In the convention where \(W\) is an endomorphism of two-forms,
Chern--Gauss--Bonnet for an Einstein four-metric gives
\[
 8\pi^2\chi(M_j)=
      \int\left(|W|^2+\frac{s^2}{24}\right)
      =\int|W|^2+6\operatorname{Vol}(M_j,g).
 \tag{8}
\]
Combining (6)--(8) would require
\[
 d_jV_N\le\operatorname{Vol}(M_j,g)
       \le d_jV_N-\frac{8\pi^2}{3},
 \tag{9}
\]
which is impossible. This excludes every Einstein constant, not merely
metrics close to a chosen gluing.

The entropy and degree input is the main theorem and its Ricci-volume
corollary on printed page 734 of
[Besson--Courtois--Gallot, GAFA 5 (1995)](https://www.aimath.org/WWN/entropyrigidity/papers/BessonCourtoisGallot.pdf).
The same comparison already underlies the inherited \(N\#N\) obstruction
in [the global manuscript](skill/research/20260921_global.tex),
Section “A fixed hyperbolic connected sum already has a gap.”
No new entropy inequality is claimed here.

## 3. A single fixed replacement, with no shrinking limit

Choose \(r>0\) so small that a radius-\(4r\) ball in \(N\) is
embedded. Select one lift of its center to every \(N_j\). Delete the
radius-\(r\) ball. Replace it by a fixed compact manifold
\[
 K=(S^1\times S^3)\setminus\operatorname{int}B^4
 \]
equipped with a fixed smooth metric \(k\) agreeing with the hyperbolic
ball metric on its boundary collar, in the boundary identification used
for (5). To construct such \(k\), prescribe the hyperbolic metric in a
collar and combine it with any interior positive-definite metric by a
partition of unity away from a smaller collar. The metrics agree exactly
near the seam, so the resulting \(g_j\) is smooth and equals \(h_j\)
outside the chosen ball.

Both the deleted ball and the inserted piece are independent of \(j\).
Consequently, with a fixed real constant \(c_V\),
\[
 \operatorname{Vol}(M_j,g_j)=d_jV_N+c_V,\qquad
 \int_{M_j}|\operatorname{Ric}_{g_j}+3g_j|^p
          =\int_K|\operatorname{Ric}_k+3k|^p=:C_p .
 \tag{10}
\]
The residual is zero on the entire exterior and on the matching collar.
Equations (2)--(3) follow.

There is also a direct bounded-geometry proof. Every point sufficiently
far from \(K\) has a fixed-radius neighborhood isometric to a
hyperbolic ball; the cover cannot shorten the base injectivity radius.
Every other point has a fixed-radius neighborhood inside the one fixed
compact model consisting of \(K\) and its radius-\(3r\) hyperbolic
buffer. A finite collection of smaller normal neighborhoods covers that
model. Taking the minimum of their admissible radii and of the exterior
hyperbolic radius proves a uniform injectivity lower bound. All curvature
derivatives are bounded on the compact model and are fixed on the
hyperbolic exterior. This proves (1). No rescaling of the replacement,
thin neck, or unproved degeneration estimate is involved.

The residual on \(K\) is not identically zero: otherwise every
constructed \(g_j\) would be Einstein with constant \(-3\), contradicting
Section 2. Its operator-norm maximum is therefore the same positive
number for every \(j\), proving (4).

## 4. Consequence for the research program

This is a completed counterexample to an *unrestricted integral
extension*, derived by fixed-piece insertion and classical comparison.
Its mechanism is dilution of a fixed, nonzero defect by growing
hyperbolic volume. It is not a new solution of the pointwise problem.

In particular, replacing Yau26's pointwise Ricci requirement by a
normalized finite-\(p\) requirement changes the problem even under
bounded curvature and a uniform injectivity radius. The successful
actual-monopole gap must retain information that rules out this type of
dilution. Adding bounded local geometry alone would not provide that
information.

The construction gives no decreasing maximum defect on \(M_j\).
Whether entirely different metrics on these varying no-Einstein
topologies can achieve pointwise defects tending to zero remains open
in this note. A lower bound tending to zero would not construct them.

[The exact checker](round18_integral_limits_check.py) checks the
Euler/volume incompatibility and the normalized defect algebra; the
existence of finite covers, degree comparison, and the smooth
fixed-piece construction are the mathematical arguments above.
