# A signature barrier to enlarging the slow-collar construction

22 September 2026.

## 1. Result and its scope

The proposed extension to arbitrary representations of a free quotient is
already contained in [Round 11](round11_closed_gluing.md), with zero circle
shear. The standard geometric free quotients were already treated in the
[earlier analysis](skill/research/20260912.tex), immediately before
`BUN:WP-signature`. This includes noncommuting and pseudo-Anosov monodromy.
No new smooth near-Einstein family is obtained by repeating that argument.

There is, however, a quantitative obstruction to extending the same
hyperbolic-base, hyperbolic-fibre, minimal-fibre construction to surface
bundles with nonzero signature. Let

\[
 \Sigma_p\longrightarrow M\stackrel\pi\longrightarrow\Sigma_q,
 \qquad p,q\ge2,\qquad \chi=4(p-1)(q-1),\quad \tau=\tau(M).
 \tag{1}
\]

Suppose \(g\) makes \(\pi\) a Riemannian submersion, every fibre has
intrinsic curvature \(-\kappa\), the base has curvature \(-\kappa\),
and every fibre is minimal, for a fixed \(\kappa>0\). There is no
restriction on base or fibre injectivity radii, connection curvature, or
monodromy. Then

\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \frac{\|g^{-1}\operatorname{Ric}_g+\kappa I\|_{\infty,\mathrm{op}}}
      {\kappa}
 \ \ge\ \frac{3|\tau|}{2\chi}.
 \quad}                                                        \tag{2}
\]

In particular, if \(\tau\ne0\), none of these metrics can form a
pointwise near-Einstein sequence on the fixed bundle. A flat connection
does not remove the obstruction. Area-preserving flat surface bundles of
nonzero signature actually exist, by Kotschick--Morita, Theorem 1 of
[*Signatures of foliated surface bundles and the symplectomorphism groups
of surfaces*](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0305182).

This is a theorem about the stated submersion geometry, not nonexistence
of Einstein metrics on \(M\). Kodaira fibrations already give nonzero
signature examples with negative Kähler--Einstein metrics; these were
explicitly retained in [Round 10](round10_bundle.md). The new deduction
here is the explicit linear signature cost for the minimal hyperbolic
submersion class, obtained by combining earlier moduli-energy and Ricci
identities, rather than a construction or a literature-priority claim.
The moduli-energy obstruction itself was already proved in
`BUN:WP-signature`; the submersion blocks and stronger exact rigidity
were already proved in `SUB:blocks`, `SUB:integrable`, and `SUB:basic`
of the earlier analysis. They are retained below as inputs, with their
conventions made explicit.

[Round 18](round18_twisted_operator.md) concerns finite-monodromy exact
quotients and does not enlarge the slow-collar topological class to
nonzero signature. Section 7 gives a short direct proof of an inherited
rigidity corollary identifying the exact metrics in the present class.

## 2. Why all free-quotient examples were already included

Fix \(\rho=\bar\rho\circ r\), where
\(r:\pi_1(\Sigma_q)\twoheadrightarrow F_s\) and
\(\bar\rho:F_s\to\operatorname{Mod}(\Sigma_p)\) is arbitrary. Realize
\(r\) by a map from the base to a rose. Choose one regular interior
point on each edge. Its inverse image is a disjoint multicurve; each
complementary component maps into the tree obtained by cutting the rose
at these points. The monodromy on each complementary component is
therefore trivial. Inessential and parallel curves can be removed or
their adjacent disc/annular product regions absorbed. The remaining
base pieces have negative Euler characteristic. The bundle is assembled
from product pieces by fixed fibre diffeomorphisms on the seams.

Equivalently, free quotients are represented geometrically by systems of
disjoint cooriented curves; see Marzantowicz--Michalak,
[*Relations between Reeb graphs, systems of hypersurfaces and epimorphisms
onto free groups*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2002.02388). The construction above
only needs triviality of the restrictions. For genus at least two,
contractibility of \(\operatorname{Diff}_0\) identifies these bundles
with their mapping-class monodromy, so no extra two-dimensional clutching
class has been omitted.

Set \(u_e=0\) in Round 11, formula (1), and choose its fibre paths from
\(h_v\) to \(f_e^*h_w\). All its complete Ricci estimates apply unchanged.
No commutativity, finite order or common invariant hyperbolic metric is
needed. The resulting fixed bundle has

\[
 \|\operatorname{Ric}_{g_\ell}+\kappa g_\ell\|_{g_\ell,\mathrm{op}}
 \longrightarrow0,
 \qquad \operatorname{Vol}(g_\ell)=\frac{4\pi^2\chi}{\kappa^2}.
 \tag{3}
\]

Its signature is zero. This follows either from Novikov additivity of
the product pieces or from \(H^2(F_s;\mathbb R)=0\) and the signature
characteristic class. Having noncommuting monodromy alone is therefore
not a test for leaving the inherited family.

## 3. A normalization-independent moduli-energy calculation

This is the inherited `BUN:WP-signature` calculation in an explicit
real-tensor normalization, not a new signature or energy formula.

It is useful to specify the metric normalization instead of silently
mixing conventions for the Weil--Petersson metric. On Teichmüller space
use the real tensor \(L^2\) metric

\[
 \mathsf G_T(k,l)=\int_F\langle k,l\rangle_{\bar h}\,dA_{\bar h},
 \quad K_{\bar h}=-1,
 \quad \operatorname{tr}_{\bar h}k=\operatorname{div}_{\bar h}k=0,
 \tag{4}
\]

and its Kähler form \(\Omega_T(k,l)=\mathsf G_T(Jk,l)\). Here \(k,l\)
are actual derivatives of the hyperbolic metric, not quadratic
differentials rescaled by an unstated constant. This is the convention
in Guillarmou--Moroianu, equations (24)--(27) and Lemma 26 of
[*Chern--Simons line bundle on Teichmüller space*](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1102.1981).
With this convention,

\[
 [\Omega_T]=8\pi^2\kappa_1,
 \qquad \kappa_1=\pi_*e(T_vM)^2.
 \tag{5}
\]

One can check the factor directly from the same vertical connection.
For unit-curvature fibre TT derivatives \(A=\bar h^{-1}k\) and
\(B=\bar h^{-1}l\), its horizontal curvature is
\(-[A,B]/4\), mixed curvature vanishes at the chosen horizontal
point, and fibre curvature is the unit rotation. Consequently
\(\int_F\operatorname{tr}(R\wedge R)=-\Omega_T\).
Since \(p_1(T_v)=-\operatorname{tr}(R\wedge R)/(8\pi^2)=e(T_v)^2\),
(5) follows. This also avoids relying on a convention for a complex
Beltrami inner product. The Chern--Weil computation concerns a universal
fibre connection and does not assume that the physical bundle connection
in (2) is the TT connection.

Let \(f\) be the equivariant fibre-moduli map on the universal cover of
the base. Its invariant tensors descend to the base; equivalently use
the moduli orbifold, keeping stabilizers. The tangent splitting
\(TM=T_vM\oplus\pi^*TB\), together with \(e(TB)^2=0\), gives

\[
 3\tau=\langle e(T_vM)^2,[M]\rangle,
 \qquad \int_B f^*\Omega_T=24\pi^2\tau.
 \tag{6}
\]

For every oriented base metric, Kähler Cauchy--Schwarz gives

\[
 |f^*\Omega_T(e_1,e_2)|
 \le\tfrac12\bigl(|df(e_1)|_T^2+|df(e_2)|_T^2\bigr),
 \quad
 \boxed{\int_B|df|_T^2\,dA_B\ge48\pi^2|\tau|.}             \tag{7}
\]

No compact-moduli or noncollapse estimate enters this bound. If the
moduli map is constant off disjoint collars and depends on only the
collar distance within each, it has rank at most one everywhere.
Then \(f^*\Omega_T=0\) and necessarily \(\tau=0\). Thus a nonzero
surface-relation class cannot be carried solely by these one-dimensional
paths. Rank-two variation must remain somewhere, even if the base
degenerates. This is the precise obstruction, not an assertion that
every zero-signature representation factors through a free group.

## 4. All Ricci blocks in the flat, area-preserving model

These specialize the earlier `SUB:blocks` and `SUB:vv-integrable` formulas.

First suppose the connection is flat and preserves a fibre area form.
In its flat charts write

\[
 g=b_{ab}(y)dy^a dy^b+h_{ij}(y,x)dx^i dx^j,
 \qquad S_a=\tfrac12h^{-1}\partial_a h,
 \quad H_a=\operatorname{tr}S_a=0.
 \tag{8}
\]

The last equality means that the area form of \(h\) is preserved in
the horizontal directions, not merely that every fibre has the same
total area. Put \(E=\operatorname{Ric}+\kappa g\). With base and
fibre both of curvature \(-\kappa\), the full Ricci defect is

\[
 \boxed{\begin{aligned}
 E_{ab}&=-\operatorname{tr}(S_aS_b),\\
 E_{ai}&=(\operatorname{div}_h S_a)_i,\\
 (E_{VV})^\sharp&=-b^{ab}D_aS_b.
 \end{aligned}}                                               \tag{9}
\]

Here \(D_aS_b=\partial_aS_b+[S_a,S_b]-\Gamma^c_{ab}S_c\) is the
covariant derivative using the metric-compatible vertical connection
and the base connection. The commutator contracts to zero but is
retained to specify the tensor derivative correctly. The remaining
entries are the symmetric transposes. These are Gauss--Codazzi formulas
in two horizontal directions. In particular

\[
 -\operatorname{tr}_H E=Q:=\sum_{a=1}^2\operatorname{tr}(S_a^2)\ge0,
 \quad \operatorname{tr}_V E=0,
 \quad s_g=-4\kappa-Q.                                     \tag{10}
\]

The mixed and vertical blocks must also tend to zero in a successful
construction. Their possible cancellation never removes the definite
horizontal block. A bound on the norm of the whole endomorphism bounds
the norm of each horizontal compression.

These models exist on the actual Kotschick--Morita bundles: fix one of
their area-preserving flat representations, and hence fix its total
space. Choose any smooth vertical metric, uniformize fibrewise to
curvature \(-\kappa\), and apply the fibrewise parametric Moser
construction to match the invariant area form, normalized to
\(4\pi(p-1)/\kappa\). All operations are smooth over the compact
base and the resulting metric family has the fixed marking. The
flat horizontal distribution then gives (8). The original existence
theorem does not specify explicit monodromy words; none are fabricated
here. Fixing any bundle supplied by that theorem is sufficient for
the stated nonvacuous obstruction.

## 5. Proof of the quantitative gap, including nonflat connections

For a general submersion let \(S_X\) be the fibre shape operator in a
horizontal direction \(X\), defined by
\(\mathcal L_Xh=2h(S_X\cdot,\cdot)\). Let
\(A_XY=\tfrac12[X,Y]^V\), with \(X,Y\) basic horizontal lifts.
Write \(|A|^2=\sum_{a,b}|A_{e_a}e_b|^2\). The minimal-fibre
horizontal Ricci identity is

\[
 E(X,Y)=-\operatorname{tr}(S_XS_Y)
          -2\sum_b\langle A_Xe_b,A_Ye_b\rangle,
 \quad -\operatorname{tr}_H E=Q+2|A|^2.                     \tag{11}
\]

For completeness, its coefficients follow from the sectional
submersion identities of O'Neill,
[*The fundamental equations of a submersion*](https://doi.org/10.1307/mmj/1028999604):
horizontal planes contribute
\(K_B-3|A_XY|^2\); summing mixed planes contributes
\(|A_X|^2-|S_X|^2\), plus the derivative of the mean-curvature
vector, which vanishes for minimal fibres. Thus the net coefficient
of the horizontal \(A\)-norm is \(-2\). Only (11) is needed for
the general-connection obstruction; (9) supplies every Ricci block
for the actual flat comparison objects.

The TT projection is orthogonal in the tensor \(L^2\) metric.
For \(\bar h=\kappa h\), the horizontal derivative has
\(|\partial_X\bar h|_{\bar h}^2=4|S_X|_h^2\) and
\(dA_{\bar h}=\kappa dA_h\). Moduli variation is its TT projection,
so the same bound holds for any connection. In a local trivialization,
a horizontal lift differentiates the fibre metric as
\(\partial_Xh+\mathcal L_Vh\). The Lie derivative is orthogonal to
every TT tensor, so its TT projection is unchanged. This is a
directionwise derivative and does not require an integral horizontal
chart when the connection is nonflat. Consequently

\[
 |df(X)|_T^2\le4\kappa\int_{F_y}|S_X|^2\,dA_h.
 \tag{12}
\]

Fibre integration of (12), followed by (7), yields

\[
 \int_M Q\,dV_g\ge\frac{12\pi^2|\tau|}{\kappa}.          \tag{13}
\]

Let \(\delta=\|g^{-1}E\|_{\infty,\mathrm{op}}\).
Equation (11) gives \(Q+2|A|^2\le2\delta\). Gauss--Bonnet and
the submersion volume form give

\[
 V=\frac{4\pi^2\chi}{\kappa^2},\qquad
 2\delta V\ge\frac{12\pi^2|\tau|}{\kappa}.
 \tag{14}
\]

This proves (2). All constants are independent of the degeneration
parameter, and the proof controls the norm in the actual four-metric.
Connection curvature only strengthens this particular obstruction.

## 6. What a possible extension must change

The base-curvature hypothesis has a useful exact replacement. Keep
minimal hyperbolic fibres of curvature \(-\kappa\), but let the
base metric be arbitrary with Gaussian curvature \(K_B\). Then

\[
 \operatorname{tr}_H E=2(K_B+\kappa)-Q-2|A|^2.
 \tag{15}
\]

The fibre area is still \(4\pi(p-1)/\kappa\). Integrating (15),
using Gauss--Bonnet on the base and (13), proves the tradeoff

\[
 \boxed{\quad
  (1+\delta/\kappa)\,\frac{V}{V_0}
       \ge1+\frac{3|\tau|}{2\chi},
  \qquad V_0=\frac{4\pi^2\chi}{\kappa^2}.
 \quad}                                                       \tag{16}
\]

Thus merely stretching more disjoint collars, while retaining the
product-normalized volume and minimal hyperbolic fibres, cannot work.
An extension must change at least one of these constraints: retain
a volume/base-curvature contribution of the size in (16), admit
nonminimal fibres and control their mean-curvature terms, change the
intrinsic fibre equation, or leave this submersion geometry entirely.
None of these alternatives has been solved here. In particular,
nonzero signature does not obstruct small Ricci defect on arbitrary
metrics: the exact Kodaira controls show the contrary.

For comparison, the earlier `BUNTOP:signature-volume` consequence of
the monopole scalar estimate applies without the submersion ansatz,
under its two-orientation symplectic and \(b_2^\pm>1\) hypotheses:
\((1+\delta/\kappa)^2V/V_0\ge1+3|\tau|/(2\chi)\).
At \(V=V_0\) it gives the square-root gap. Equation (16) has a single
power and hence the linear gap (2), but uses the additional minimal
hyperbolic-fibre geometry. The stronger constant is not a removal of
those geometric hypotheses.

For a fixed surface relation of signature zero which does not factor
through a free group, (7) gives no positive lower bound. This note
does not claim its metric construction or its impossibility. That is
a distinct next topology test, rather than a consequence of
noncommutativity or of this signature argument.

## 7. An inherited exact-rigidity corollary and a direct proof

The earlier `SUB:basic` theorem already treats every negative Einstein
surface submersion with basic fibre mean curvature, including minimal
fibres, without imposing intrinsic fibre curvature. The following is
its narrower corollary, not a new classification. Its direct proof
illustrates the role of the same vertical and mixed blocks.

**Corollary.** Suppose an Einstein metric \(\operatorname{Ric}_g=-\kappa g\)
on (1) makes \(\pi\) a Riemannian submersion with minimal fibres whose
intrinsic curvature is \(-\kappa\). No curvature condition is imposed
on the base. Then \(g\) is locally the product of two surfaces of
curvature \(-\kappa\), and the bundle has finite isometry monodromy.
Conversely finite-monodromy bundles admit such product-quotient metrics.

**Proof.** For minimal fibres, the scalar submersion identity is

\[
 s_g=2K_B-2\kappa-|A|^2-Q.
 \tag{17}
\]

See also Lott, equation (2.1) of
[*Remark about scalar curvature and Riemannian submersions*](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0504421),
with the fibre mean-curvature vector zero. Subtract the horizontal
Ricci trace, which is \(2K_B-Q-2|A|^2\) by (11). This gives the
pointwise identity

\[
 \operatorname{tr}_V\operatorname{Ric}_g=-2\kappa+|A|^2.
 \tag{18}
\]

The Einstein equation forces \(A=0\). The horizontal distribution is
therefore integrable, and its flat local trivializations put the metric
in (8). Minimality gives \(\operatorname{tr}S_X=0\), and the mixed
Einstein equation in (9) gives \(\operatorname{div}_hS_X=0\). For a
fixed base tangent vector \(X\), the symmetric tensor
\(h(S_X\cdot,\cdot)\) is thus the real part of a holomorphic quadratic
differential on the closed fibre. The horizontal Einstein equation says

\[
 \operatorname{tr}(S_X^2)=K_B+\kappa
 \quad\text{for every unit }X.
 \tag{19}
\]

Its right side is constant along the fibre. A nonzero holomorphic
quadratic differential on a closed genus-\(p\ge2\) surface has zeros,
since its zero divisor has degree \(4p-4>0\). Its norm cannot be a
nonzero constant. Hence (19) forces \(S_X=0\) for every \(X\), and
then \(K_B=-\kappa\). The fibres are totally geodesic and their metric
is parallel in the flat connection. Parallel transport therefore has
image in the finite orientation-preserving isometry group of a fixed
closed hyperbolic fibre. Passing to the finite cover killing that
holonomy gives an actual product metric. The converse is the classical
finite-group product quotient, using an invariant hyperbolic fibre
metric. This proves the theorem.

This inherited exact statement also follows directly from classical
submersion identities and the surface TT description. It excludes an exact adapted solution even
for the zero-signature infinite-monodromy examples of Round 11. It is
compatible with their approximate metrics: neither small mixed Ricci
error nor degenerating fibre geometry has been upgraded to the exact
TT/constant-norm equations used in the proof. It also does not make
the existence problem for arbitrary metrics on those smooth manifolds
an adapted-submersion problem.

## 8. Finite verification

`round20_construction_check.py` recomputes the full coordinate Ricci
matrix for a two-base-variable hyperbolic fibre family of fixed area,
checks all blocks of (9), rejects a missing mixed block and a missing
horizontal quadratic term, and checks the finite matrix normalization
behind (5) and the arithmetic of (14)--(16). The two fibre directions
in that local model are diffeomorphism variations; it is a tensor
identity test, not a model with nonzero moduli energy or signature.
Global topology, the signature formula, and the TT projection argument
are the mathematical proof above and are not certified by that script.

## 9. A fixed zero-signature target with faithful Torelli monodromy

The signature-zero remainder is not empty. The following construction
uses established subgroup theorems to specify an actual smooth bundle
whose chosen monodromy cannot factor through any free group. It does
not supply near-Einstein metrics, and it does not classify all other
decompositions of its total space.

**Target theorem.** There is a smooth area-preserving representation

\[
 \widetilde\rho:\pi_1(\Sigma_2)\longrightarrow
        \operatorname{Symp}(\Sigma_6,\omega;D)
 \tag{20}
\]

whose diffeomorphisms fix a disk \(D\) pointwise, and whose projection
\(\rho\) to \(\operatorname{Mod}(\Sigma_6)\) is injective with image
in the subgroup generated by separating twists, hence in Torelli.
Its suspension

\[
 M_\rho=(\widetilde\Sigma_2\times\Sigma_6)/\pi_1(\Sigma_2)
       \longrightarrow\Sigma_2
 \tag{21}
\]

is a closed oriented smooth flat surface bundle, with a section of
self-intersection zero and

\[
 \chi(M_\rho)=20,\qquad \tau(M_\rho)=0,\qquad
 b_1=16,\qquad b_2=50,\qquad b_2^+=b_2^-=25.
 \tag{22}
\]

The monodromy of this projection, and of every finite base-cover
pullback, cannot factor through a free group.

**Five separating curves.** Start with a disk containing five small
inner disks, arranged at the vertices of a pentagon, and remove their
interiors. Attach a once-punctured torus to each of the six boundary
components, including the outer one. The result is a closed genus-six
surface. For \(i\in\mathbb Z/5\), let \(c_i\) enclose the two inner
holes \(i,i+1\) and a narrow strip along the corresponding pentagon
edge. After the tori are attached, \(c_i\) separates a genus-two side
from a genus-four side. Curves from nonadjacent pentagon edges have
disjoint representatives, chosen simultaneously using narrow strips.

Curves from adjacent edges cannot be isotoped disjoint: their
genus-two sides contain overlapping, nonnested sets of the independent
torus homology summands. If two separating curves were disjoint, their
chosen sides would be nested, disjoint, or together exhaust the surface.
Each alternative contradicts those homology subspaces; the last also
contradicts their dimensions. Thus the disjointness graph \(\Gamma\)
of the five curves is the complement of a pentagon, which is again
\(C_5\). The genus-one summand attached to the outer boundary contains
a common fixed disk disjoint from all twist supports.

**An actual lifted group action.** Let \(A(\Gamma)\) denote the
right-angled Artin group with one generator \(v_i\) per curve and
commutation exactly for disjoint pairs. Koberda's Theorem 1.1 in
[*Right-angled Artin groups and a generalized isomorphism problem for
finitely generated subgroups of mapping class groups*](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1007.1118)
gives \(N_0\) such that, for each fixed \(N\ge N_0\),

\[
 v_i\longmapsto T_{c_i}^{N}
 \quad\text{embeds }A(\Gamma)\text{ into }
       \operatorname{Mod}(\Sigma_6).
 \tag{23}
\]

Fix such an integer \(N\) once; it is part of the topology data, not
a metric-degeneration parameter. Choose annular, area-preserving
representatives of the five twists for one fixed area form \(\omega\).
For disjoint curves their annular supports are disjoint, so the
representatives commute as diffeomorphisms, not only up to isotopy.
The defining relations of \(A(\Gamma)\) therefore give a homomorphism
to \(\operatorname{Symp}(\Sigma_6,\omega;D)\), and it is injective
because its mapping-class projection (23) is injective. Separating
twists act trivially on integral first homology.

Crisp--Wiest's explicit genus-two dissection in §4, Figure 2, of
[*Embeddings of graph braid and surface groups in right-angled Artin
groups and braid groups*](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0303217)
gives an embedding
\(j:\pi_1(\Sigma_2)\hookrightarrow A(C_5)\): their labelled cubical
map to the Salvetti complex is a local isometry. Identify their
pentagon with \(\Gamma\), fix this \(j\), and compose with the
actual diffeomorphism representation above. This proves (20).
In particular, the surface relation is an exact identity of
diffeomorphisms; there is no unproved Nielsen-realization step.

**Topology and the free-quotient obstruction.** The fixed disk supplies
the trivial disk subbundle \(\Sigma_2\times D\), so its central
section has trivial normal bundle. Its section also splits the
fundamental-group extension. Since the action on fibre first homology
is trivial, abelianization gives
\(H_1(M_\rho;\mathbb Z)=\mathbb Z^{12}\oplus\mathbb Z^4\).
Euler characteristic is multiplicative. The signature vanishes by
the Chern--Hirzebruch--Serre signature-multiplicativity theorem, since
the action on all fibre cohomology is trivial; see
[*On the index of a fibered manifold*](https://webhomes.maths.ed.ac.uk/~v1ranick/papers/chs.pdf).
Poincaré duality then proves the remaining numbers in (22).

If \(\rho\) factored through a free group, its injectivity would force
an embedding of \(\pi_1(\Sigma_2)\) in that free group. Subgroups of
free groups are free, whereas a closed hyperbolic surface group is not
free. The same proof applies after a finite base cover, whose group
is still a closed hyperbolic surface group and on which \(\rho\)
remains injective. Thus the free-quotient construction of Section 2
does not apply to this marked bundle projection, even virtually.

**Precisely what remains open.** The result specifies a flat,
zero-signature, faithful-monodromy target; the signature gap (2)
vanishes on it. An exact minimal-fibre Einstein metric adapted to
this projection is excluded by the inherited rigidity in Section 7.
Existence of a pointwise near-Einstein sequence on its fixed smooth
total space is not proved here. Nor is it proved that \(M_\rho\)
cannot admit a different surface fibration or a product-block
decomposition of the general sheared Round-11 type. The faithful
monodromy argument controls the specified projection only. Establishing
that stronger diffeomorphism-type distinction is a separate topology
problem; without it this must not be called a new smooth class outside
Round 11.

The finite check also verifies the five-curve incidence graph and the
arithmetic in (22). The faithful subgroup and smooth lift are proved
above using the cited group theorems; a graph computation alone would
not verify either assertion.



---

## 原稿 15: 20260912.tex

来源：`mathematics/current/skill/research/20260912.tex`；范围：`lines 3283-3476`。

原文件 SHA-256：`a472ae0c15321dd3d99e792f89afa0322be416a65aeac526b51e1f97930b555f`。

E_D 规范、BUN:slow 全节及 BUN:ricci-matrix 推导。此为标注范围的原文摘录，不是全文或新证明。

````latex
\chapter{固定曲面丛上的全局逼近、修正与退化}
\label{BUN:chapter}

本章围绕一个固定的光滑闭四维流形展开：纤维与基底均为高亏格曲面，单值变换由一次非分离 Dehn twist 生成。已经完成的核心构造是：\emph{在同一个流形上，全局最大 Ricci 误差可以趋于零，且完整曲率及任意固定阶导数可以保持有界。} 这并不是原问题的反例，因为尚未排除这个流形的所有 Einstein 度量。随后建立的三阶修正、全局配对、共形障碍和谱计算，各自说明了特定修正机制的能力与边界。

以下固定纤维亏格 $p\ge2$、基底亏格 $q\ge2$。归一化目标始终是 $\operatorname{Ric}=-g$，并记
\begin{equation}
 \Phi(g)=\operatorname{Ric}_g+g,\qquad
 \delta(g)=\sup_M|g^{-1}\Phi(g)|_{\mathrm{op}},\qquad
 V_0=16\pi^2(p-1)(q-1).
 \label{BUN:normalization}
\end{equation}
张量的 $C^k$ 范数指相应背景下 $0$ 至 $k$ 阶协变导数的上确界之和；$|\cdot|$ 无特别说明时为 Hilbert--Schmidt 范数。对称矩阵的核范数 $|Q|_*$ 指绝对特征值之和，它是算子范数配对所需的对偶范数。所有渐近常数允许依赖固定的曲面、轮廓和导数阶数，不能改称仅依赖维数的常数。

\section{不退化纤维的慢插值：一个完整的闭流形构造}
\label{BUN:slow}

\subsection{拓扑先固定，度量再变化}
在闭双曲纤维 $(F,h_0)$ 上固定非分离简单闭测地线及其子领圈
\[
 [-R_0,R_0]\times\mathbb R/\mathbb Z,
 \qquad h_0=du^2+a(u)^2ds^2,\qquad a(u)=a_0\cosh u.
\]
这里 $a_0,R_0,h_0$ 完全固定。取光滑 $\zeta$，在领圈左、右端邻域分别恒为 $0,1$。映射
\[
 D(u,s)=(u,s+\zeta(u))
\]
在领圈两端均为恒等，故零延拓为纤维上的 Dehn twist。定义
\begin{equation}
 h_z=du^2+a^2(ds+z\zeta' du)^2\quad(0\le z\le1),
 \qquad h_z=h_0\quad\text{在领圈外}.
 \label{BUN:fiber-path}
\end{equation}
每个 $z$ 对应的局部代换 $s'=s+z\zeta(u)$ 给出
\begin{equation}
 K_{h_z}=-1,\qquad dA_{h_z}=dA_{h_0},\qquad h_1=D^*h_0.
 \label{BUN:path-properties}
\end{equation}
非整数 $z$ 的坐标代换不必延拓成闭纤维的微分同胚；已经匹配边界的是度量本身。这避免了把非平凡映射类错误地同痕化。

在闭基底 $B$ 上固定非分离曲线 $c$。沿它切开，取补集上的平凡纤维丛；将连接领圈的两端分别用恒等和 $D$ 粘接。得到固定闭流形 $E_D$。其单值变换表示通过
\[
 \pi_1(B)\longrightarrow\mathbb Z\longrightarrow\operatorname{Mod}(F),
 \qquad 1\longmapsto[D].
\]

令基底双曲度量中的 $c$ 长度为 $\ell\downarrow0$，其余 Fenchel--Nielsen 参数固定。标准领圈半宽为
\begin{equation}
 w(x)=\operatorname{arsinh}\frac1{\sinh(x/2)},\qquad
 L=\tfrac12w(\ell),\qquad
 g_B=dt^2+\ell^2\cosh^2t\,d\theta^2\quad(|t|\le L).
 \label{BUN:collar}
\end{equation}
领圈公式与单曲线尖点退化是经典输入。\footnote{M.~Rupflin and P.~Topping, \emph{Horizontal curves of hyperbolic metrics}, Appendix A, Lemma A.1 与 Theorem A.4：\url{https://arxiv.org/pdf/1605.06691}. 本章使用它们提供的双曲领圈和厚部收敛；Ricci 矩阵与修正均在下文计算。}
取固定端部常值轮廓 $\eta:[-1,1]\to[0,1]$，置 $z(t)=\eta(t/L)$，并定义
\begin{equation}
 G_\ell=dt^2+\ell^2\cosh^2t\,d\theta^2+h_{z(t)}
 \quad\text{在基底领圈内};\qquad
 G_\ell=g_B+h_0\quad\text{在外部}.
 \label{BUN:slow-metric}
\end{equation}
右端内侧度量是 $D^*h_0$，等于外部度量的粘接拉回；$z$ 在端部常值，所有横向导数都匹配。因此这是整个固定 $E_D$ 上的光滑正定度量。

\subsection{全部 Ricci 块，而非仅对角估计}
记
\[
 b=\ell\cosh t,\quad j=\tanh u,\quad k=\tanh t,
 \quad Q=a\zeta',\quad v=Qz'.
\]
采用正交余标架
\[
 (e^0,e^1,e^2,e^3)=(dt,b\,d\theta,du,a(ds+z\zeta' du)).
\]
其非零结构方程为
\[
 de^1=k e^0\wedge e^1,\qquad
 de^3=j e^2\wedge e^3+v e^0\wedge e^2.
\]
纤维在 $t$ 方向的形状算子及其协变导数为
\begin{equation}
 S=\frac12\begin{pmatrix}0&v\\v&0\end{pmatrix},\quad
 \operatorname{tr}S=0,\quad
 D_tS=\begin{pmatrix}v^2/2&v_t/2\\v_t/2&-v^2/2\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad \operatorname{div}_{h_z}S=\frac{v_u+2jv}{2}e^3.
 \label{BUN:shape}
\end{equation}
$D_tS$ 中的二次对角项来自标架旋转，不能用矩阵条目的普通导数代替。切片 Gauss--Codazzi 公式给出完整矩阵
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{
 \Phi(G_\ell)_{\mathrm{ON}}=
 \begin{pmatrix}
 -v^2/2&0&0&(v_u+2jv)/2\\
 0&0&0&0\\
 0&0&-v^2/2&-(v_t+kv)/2\\
 (v_u+2jv)/2&0&-(v_t+kv)/2&v^2/2
 \end{pmatrix}.}
 \label{BUN:ricci-matrix}
\end{equation}
亦可把 $e^3$ 看成圆周连接：圆周长度函数为 $a$，连接曲率为 $(v/a)dt\wedge du$。水平曲率平方项给两个 $-v^2/2$，圆周块给 $+v^2/2$，连接曲率散度给两个混合项，得到相同公式。

令
\[
 A_0=\|Q\|_\infty,\quad A_1=\|Q'+2jQ\|_\infty,
 \quad M_i=\|\eta^{(i)}\|_\infty\ (i=1,2).
\]
矩阵的非对角部分只有两条独立边，其算子范数为对应条目平方和的平方根。因此
\begin{equation}
 \delta(G_\ell)\le
 \frac{A_0^2M_1^2}{2L^2}+
 \frac1{2L}\sqrt{(A_1M_1)^2+A_0^2(M_1+M_2/L)^2}
 \longrightarrow0.
 \label{BUN:slow-bound}
\end{equation}
领圈交叉区域外只要 $Q=0$ 或 $z'=0$，误差就严格为零；故这是全局最大误差估计。

短基底圆周没有引入隐藏的 $\ell^{-1}$：系数与 $\theta$ 无关，联络只含 $b'/b=\tanh t$。固定纤维的有限坐标覆盖与固定路径给任意固定阶导数界。对结构方程逐次协变微分得到
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 \operatorname{Vol}(G_\ell)&=V_0, & s_{G_\ell}&=-4-v^2/2,\\
 \|\nabla^m\operatorname{Rm}_{G_\ell}\|_\infty&\le C_m,
 &\|\nabla^m\Phi(G_\ell)\|_\infty&\le C_m/L.
 \end{aligned}
 \label{BUN:slow-geometric}
\end{equation}
这些高阶估计是显式构造的性质，不是一般 Ricci 夹逼的推论。

\subsection{实际退化与构造内部的障碍}
矩阵\eqref{BUN:ricci-matrix}对应的联络还给
\begin{equation}
 K(e_0,e_3)=v^2/4>0\quad\text{当 }v\ne0.
 \label{BUN:positive-plane}
\end{equation}
因此本构造并不满足严格负截面曲率。基底中央的非平凡短圆周给 $\operatorname{inj}_{\min}\le\ell/2$，而投影不增加长度，故直径发散。插值区域体积为
\[
 8\pi(p-1)\ell\sinh L=O(\sqrt\ell)\longrightarrow0.
\]
厚部光滑极限是 $\Sigma_p\times\Sigma_{q-1,2}$，体积仍为 $V_0$。非平凡粘接被送向越来越远的细颈；最大误差趋零的依据仍是\eqref{BUN:slow-bound}，不能用小体积替代。

若\eqref{BUN:slow-metric}本身为任意常数的 Einstein 度量，$e_1$ 分量先迫使 Einstein 常数为 $-1$，$e_0$ 分量再迫使 $v=0$，与两个非平凡轮廓矛盾。这只排除了该分层形式内的精确解。

同一机制适用于标准几何自由商。若曲面群生成元满足
\[
 \rho(a_i)=1,\qquad\rho(b_i)=[\phi_i]\quad(1\le i\le q),
\]
则可沿互不相交的 $a_i$ 领圈独立插值，$[\phi_i]$ 无需交换。先取从 $h_0$ 到 $(\phi_i^0)^*h_0$ 的端部常值双曲路径 $k_{i,z}$。其面积形式 $\mu_{i,z}$ 的导数正合，选择 $d\alpha_{i,z}=\partial_z\mu_{i,z}$，以
\[
 \iota_{X_{i,z}}\mu_{i,z}=-\alpha_{i,z},\qquad
 \partial_z\psi_{i,z}=X_{i,z}\circ\psi_{i,z}
\]
定义流，则 $h_{i,z}=\psi_{i,z}^*k_{i,z}$ 具有共同面积形式。终点是 $(\phi_i^0\circ\psi_{i,1})^*h_0$，映射类未变，丛总空间可用领圈内的同痕逐块识别。完整切片公式为
\[
 \Phi_{tt}=-\operatorname{tr}S_i^2,\quad \Phi_{\theta\theta}^{\mathrm{ON}}=0,
 \quad \Phi_{FF}^{\sharp}=-D_tS_i-kS_i,
 \quad \Phi_{tF}=\operatorname{div}_{h_i}S_i.
\]
有限条固定路径仍给\eqref{BUN:slow-geometric}。表示经自由群延拓到把体，丛定向地界一个五维曲面丛，因而签名为零；非交换单值变换没有在此机制中产生非零签名。

\subsection{指数积分误差与非零签名的结构限制}
若慢轮廓的变化支撑在 $|t|\le\alpha L$，固定 $0<\alpha<1$，则
$\ell=4e^{-2L}+O(e^{-6L})$，误差支持体积为 $O(e^{-(2-\alpha)L})$。
对每个有限 $r\ge1$，
\[
 \|\Phi(G_L)\|_{L^r}\le C_rL^{-1}e^{-(2-\alpha)L/r},
 \qquad\|s_{G_L}+4\|_{L^r}\le C_rL^{-2}e^{-(2-\alpha)L/r}.
\]
记 $q_L=|S|^2$、$A_L=\int q_L$、$B_L=\int q_L^2$，则
$A_L=O(L^{-2}e^{-(2-\alpha)L})$、$B_L\le CL^{-2}A_L$。
由 $s=-4-q_L$ 与 Yamabe 商的 Hölder 下界、常数函数上界，
\[
 -\sqrt{16V_0+8A_L+B_L}\le Y([G_L])
 \le-4\sqrt{V_0}-A_L/\sqrt{V_0}.
\]
一旦使用本章拓扑部分证明的 $Y(E_D)=-4\sqrt{V_0}$，即得
\[
 Y(E_D)-Y([G_L])=\frac{A_L}{\sqrt{V_0}}[1+O(L^{-2})]
 \quad(A_L>0).
\]
这量化逼近而不宣称上确界实现。

对任意高亏格曲面丛 $E\to B$，取单值变换等变的纤维模参数映射
$F:\widetilde B\to\mathcal T_p$。采用
$[\omega_{\mathrm{WP}}]=2\pi^2\kappa_1$ 的规范，签名公式给
\begin{equation}
 \int_B F^*\omega_{\mathrm{WP}}=6\pi^2\tau(E),
 \qquad\frac12\int_B|dF|^2dA\ge6\pi^2|\tau(E)|.
 \label{BUN:WP-signature}
\end{equation}
第一式由 $\kappa_1=\pi_*(e(T_vE)^2)$ 与 $3\tau=\langle p_1(TE),[E]\rangle$ 得到；第二式由 Kähler 形式的面积不等式得到。\footnote{S.~Wolpert, \emph{Chern forms and the Riemann tensor for the moduli space of curves}, 特征形式规范见印刷第120页及第5章：\url{https://www.math.umd.edu/~swolpert/preprints/Chern_forms.pdf}. 使用等变映射或先取无挠有限覆盖，避免粗模空间遗漏单值变换信息。}
当 $K_B=-1$ 时，
\[
 \sup_B|dF|^2\ge\frac{3\pi|\tau(E)|}{q-1}.
\]
所以非零签名的固定亏格丛不能保持基底曲率 $-1$，同时令所有方向的模参数速度一致趋零。自由商构造的每个领圈只沿一个变量变化，故 $F^*\omega_{\mathrm{WP}}=0$；其签名为零是结构事实。这个能量限制不排除一般非下沉度量中的曲率抵消。


````

## 原稿 16: 20260912.tex

来源：`mathematics/current/skill/research/20260912.tex`；范围：`lines 4038-4332`。

原文件 SHA-256：`a472ae0c15321dd3d99e792f89afa0322be416a65aeac526b51e1f97930b555f`。

BUNTOP:object 与 BUNTOP:covers 的实际对象及覆盖论证。此为标注范围的原文摘录，不是全文或新证明。

````latex
\section{曲面丛候选的拓扑、精确全局不变量与 Einstein 必要条件}
\label{BUNTOP:section}

本节固定一个确已具有任意紧负 Ricci 夹逼序列的闭四维候选，
并把它的拓扑结论、变分结论和精确 Einstein 方程的条件结论分开。
这些材料的共同限制是：\emph{尚未排除该流形上的所有 Einstein 度量。}

\subsection{固定对象与拓扑识别}
\label{BUNTOP:object}

令 \(F=\Sigma_p\)、\(B=\Sigma_q\)，\(p,q\ge2\)。
在 \(F\) 上取非分离简单闭曲线 \(\alpha\)，令 \(D\) 为其正 Dehn twist；
选择支集外的固定基点。令 \(u:\pi_1(B)\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb Z\) 为本原满同态。
本文的 \(E_D\) 是单值变换
\[
 \rho(\gamma)=[D]^{u(\gamma)}
\]
所定义的光滑定向曲面丛。记
\begin{equation}
 G=\pi_1(E_D)=\Pi_p\rtimes_{D_*^{\,u(\cdot)}}\Pi_q,\qquad
 \Pi_r=\pi_1(\Sigma_r),\qquad
 c=\chi(E_D),\qquad V_0=4\pi^2c .
 \label{BUNTOP:group}
\end{equation}
支集外固定点给出截面，从而半直积写法有实际几何依据。
纤维、基底均非球面，纤维丛同伦正合列使 \(E_D\) 也是
\(K(G,1)\)。直接计算得到
\begin{equation}
 \chi(E_D)=4(p-1)(q-1),\qquad
 \tau(E_D)=0,\qquad
 H_1(E_{D^k};\mathbb Z)
 \cong\mathbb Z^{\,2p+2q-1}\oplus\mathbb Z/|k|\mathbb Z
 \quad(k\ne0).
 \label{BUNTOP:invariants}
\end{equation}
这里 \(b_1(E_D)=2p+2q-1\) 为奇数，两个取向上的 \(b_+\) 均大于 \(1\)。

签名计算不依赖对手术图的猜测。
将 \(E_D\) 写成三维映射环面 \(T_D\to S^1\) 沿 \(B\to S^1\) 的拉回。
若 \(V\) 是垂直切丛，则 \(e(V)^2\) 从三维空间拉回，故为零。
再用
\[
 TE_D\cong V\oplus\pi^*TB,\qquad
 p_1(TE_D)=e(V)^2+\pi^*e(TB)^2
\]
和签名定理即得 \(\tau=0\)。一阶同调则由分裂群扩张的交换化给出：
\(H_1(E_{D^k})=H_1(B)\oplus\operatorname{coker}(D_*^k-I)\)，
非分离扭转的同调作用是秩一 transvection。

面积保持的单值代表给两个取向上的辛形式
\(\omega_\pm=\mu_F\pm\mu_B\)。相应典范类可选为
\begin{equation}
 a_+=-e_V-e_B,\qquad a_-=-e_V+e_B,\qquad
 e_V=e(V),\quad e_B=\pi^*e(TB).
 \label{BUNTOP:canonical}
\end{equation}
它们的平方在各自取向下均为 \(2c\)；
由 Taubes 非消失，它们分别是各取向的实际 monopole 类。
反取向类不能并入原取向的 monopole 类集合。

\paragraph{经典手术识别。}
取乘积 \(F\times B\) 中的 Lagrangian 环面
\(T=\alpha\times\gamma\)，其中 \(\gamma\) 是 \(u\) 的 Poincar\'e 对偶曲线。
在乘积 framing 中记经向为 \(a,b\)、meridian 为 \(\mu\)。
将新填充盘的边界指定为 \(\mu+ka\) 的 \(1/k\) Luttinger 填充，
局部等价于沿基底割线重新粘接
\((s,v)\mapsto(s+k\zeta(v),v)\)，故所得流形为 \(E_{D^{\pm k}}\)。
正负号由 meridian 与 twist 取向约定决定。
这是局部手术模型的识别；不能把流形的拓扑类型当作新发现。
Baldridge--Kirk 的 Lemma 2 提供这一机制的经典先例，
但该引理的逐字陈述处理的是其文中的环面例子，
当前高亏格识别仍由上述局部模型支撑。%
\footnote{\url{https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0703065}，Baldridge--Kirk，
\emph{Constructions of small symplectic 4-manifolds using Luttinger surgery}，§2.1、Lemma 2。}

\paragraph{适用范围的拓扑筛选。}
任意 surface-by-surface 群中的可解子群 \(S\) 满足
\[
 1\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow S\longrightarrow B'
 \longrightarrow1,\qquad A,B'\in\{1,\mathbb Z\}.
\]
因为闭高亏格曲面群的可解子群至多循环。
因此 \(S\) 虚阿贝尔、Hirsch 秩至多 \(2\)：
尤其 \(G\) 不含 \(\mathbb Z^3\)、非阿贝尔离散 Heisenberg 群或 Sol 三维格点群。
这排除了将具有这些\emph{单射外围子群}的图流形核心直接认作 \(E_D\) 的可能。
在商群里发现 Heisenberg 结构不等于原群含有该子群。

Sung 的乘积环面纤维和定理不能仅凭手术名称直接移用。
其 meridian 粘接为 \(\mu_1\mapsto-\mu_2\)，不同于上述 Luttinger 斜率。
从同一 \(F\times B\) 出发，只与 \(S^1\times X_j\) 作该定理允许的外部纤维和，
且手术圆在 \(H_1(X_j;\mathbb Q)\) 中非零时，
Mayer--Vietoris 给
\[
 b_1(\widetilde M)
 =2p+2q+\sum_j\bigl(b_1(X_j)-1\bigr)\ge2p+2q,
\]
故不能得到单 twist 的 \(E_D\)。
只作非空内部纤维和时，乘积环面补空间的边界
\(\mathbb Z^3\) 在基本群上单射，HNN 正规形使其在粘接后保留，
也不能得到高亏格曲面丛。
这两项排除不覆盖任意混合手术序列。%
\footnote{\url{https://arxiv.org/pdf/0710.2375}，Sung，
\emph{Surgery, Yamabe invariant, and Seiberg--Witten theory}，Theorem 1.5。
该定理不是任意 Luttinger 手术保持 Yamabe 值的声明。}

还有一个不依赖单值形式的刚性筛选：
\begin{equation}
 \Sigma_p\longrightarrow E\longrightarrow\Sigma_q,\quad
 E\simeq\Sigma_r\times\Sigma_s
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 E\cong_{\mathrm{diff}}\Sigma_r\times\Sigma_s .
 \label{BUNTOP:product-rigidity}
\end{equation}
证明中，纤维正规群 \(N\) 位于 \(A\times C=\Pi_r\times\Pi_s\)。
\(N\cap A\) 与 \(N\cap C\) 是 \(N\) 中逐点交换的正规子群；
曲面群中非平凡正规子群的中心化子平凡，故至少一者为 \(1\)。
不妨 \(N\cap A=1\)。与 \(A\) 取交换子便得 \(N\subset C\)；
商群 \(A\times(C/N)\) 是曲面群，因而 \(C/N=1\)。
所以纤维群为整个乘积因子，单值化平凡。
最后由 \(\operatorname{Diff}_0^+(\Sigma_p)\) 可缩，
光滑曲面丛分类给出光滑乘积。%
\footnote{\url{https://doi.org/10.4310/jdg/1214428816}，
Earle--Eells，\emph{A fibre bundle description of Teichm\"uller theory}。}
因此不能在保持此光滑曲面丛条件的同时，
自由换入一个假想的 exotic 乘积光滑结构。

\paragraph{保持已证明输入范围的两种拓扑操作。}
对于单值表示通过标准几何自由群商的更广构造族，
若全部生成元在纤维的小圆盘上恒等，
可沿法向丛平凡的截面作逐纤维连接和。
从纤维亏格 \(p_1,p_2\) 得
\[
 p_{\mathrm{new}}=p_1+p_2,\qquad
 \chi(E_{\mathrm{new}})=4(p_1+p_2-1)(q-1),\qquad
 \tau(E_{\mathrm{new}})=0 .
\]
新的单值仍通过同一自由群商，故可重新应用慢插值构造。

若给定连通 \(d\) 重纤维循环分歧覆盖，分歧点共 \(r\) 个且完全分歧，
并\emph{另外假设每个单值生成元都可提升}，
则新纤维亏格 \(P\) 满足
\[
 P-1=d(p-1)+\frac{r(d-1)}2,\qquad
 \chi(\widehat E)=4(P-1)(q-1),\qquad \tau(\widehat E)=0.
\]
自由群关系使各提升可以分别选择，
新总空间仍在已证明输入范围内。
必须在新光滑丛上重新构造度量；
分歧映射的直接度量拉回会退化，不能使用。
这两种操作扩展候选范围，但仍不产生非零签名或异常球。

相反，依靠非平凡吹胀得到的无 Einstein 证书
不能直接移入这个范围：
aspherical 曲面丛没有非零同调的异常二球。
同样，即使另一构造实现相同基本群，
仍需核对总空间的同伦型和光滑丛结构。
对于实际为 Kodaira 纤维化的复几何分歧覆盖，
负 K\"ahler--Einstein 存在性也不能被单值复杂性否定。
这些是保持输入的构造操作与排错条件，没有组成完整反例。

\subsection{有限覆盖上的复结构与非正曲率障碍}
\label{BUNTOP:covers}

\paragraph{任意有限覆盖均无复结构。}
设 \(H\le G\) 有限指数，\(N=\Pi_p\)，
\(K=H\cap N\)，\(Q'\) 为 \(H\) 在 \(\Pi_q\) 中的像。
若 \(a=[N:K]\)、\(b=[\Pi_q:Q']\)，则覆盖度为 \(d=ab\)。
对应覆盖仍是连通光滑曲面丛
\[
 \Sigma_{p'}\longrightarrow\widehat E\longrightarrow\Sigma_{q'},
 \qquad p'=1+a(p-1),\quad q'=1+b(q-1),\quad
 \tau(\widehat E)=0.
\]
具体地，原投影提升为到 \(Q'\) 对应基底覆盖的 proper submersion；
基本群满射保证纤维连通，Ehresmann 定理给出上述丛结构。

覆盖的单值像仍无限。取 \(\gamma\in H\)，使其基底像的
\(u\)-值 \(n\ne0\)；共轭作用 \(\alpha\in\operatorname{Aut}(N)\)
保持 \(K\)，其外作用为 \(D^n\)。
若覆盖单值像有限，则存在 \(m\ge1,k\in K\)，使
\(\alpha^m|_K=\operatorname{Inn}(k)|_K\)。
曲面群具有唯一根性质，而每个 \(x\in N\) 的某个正幂属于 \(K\)，故
\[
 \beta|_K=\mathrm{id},\quad
 \beta=\operatorname{Inn}(k^{-1})\alpha^m
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \beta(x)^r=x^r\Longrightarrow\beta(x)=x.
\]
于是 \(\alpha^m\) 在 \(N\) 上内，矛盾于非分离 twist 的非平凡同调作用
\(x\mapsto x\pm nm(x\cdot[\alpha])[\alpha]\)。

Kotschick 的定理准确断言：
高亏格曲面丛承载复结构且签名为零，
当且仅当其给定单值表示的像有限；
无需预先假设原投影全纯。%
\footnote{\url{https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/9911255}，Kotschick，
\emph{On regularly fibered complex surfaces}，Proposition 1、Theorem 4。}
因此
\begin{equation}
 \text{每个连通有限覆盖 }\widehat E\to E_D
 \text{ 都不承载可积复结构，两个取向均如此。}
 \label{BUNTOP:no-complex-cover}
\end{equation}

\paragraph{更强的群论结论及其独立证明。}
不能直接从四维无复结构推出群不是 K\"ahler 群。
这里另有严格的覆盖构造：
\begin{equation}
 \forall H\le_{\mathrm{f.i.}}G,\quad
 \exists H'\le_{\mathrm{f.i.}}H,\qquad b_1(H')\text{ 为奇数}.
 \label{BUNTOP:odd-deeper-cover}
\end{equation}
令 \(N_0\triangleleft G\) 是 \(H\) 的有限指数正规核心，
置 \(K_0=N_0\cap\Pi_p\)、\(B_0=N_0\cap\Pi_q\)。
二者有限指数，且
\(K_0\rtimes B_0\le N_0\le H\)。
正规性使 \(K_0\) 在 \(D_*\) 下不变；
取提升 twist 的适当正幂，并把 \(B_0\) 再限制到
\(u^{-1}(m\mathbb Z)\)，便使提升单值为
\(\Phi=\prod_iT_{\gamma_i}^{a_i}\)，其中 \(\gamma_i\) 不交、
\(a_i>0\)，且在切割块上恒等。
设切割对偶图为 \(\Gamma\)，\(r=b_1(\Gamma)\)。
非分离曲线 \(\alpha\) 的交数同态 \(\Pi_p\to\mathbb Z\)
在有限指数 \(K_0\) 上仍非零；
它在各切割块上为零，故通过对偶图因子化，证明 \(r\ge1\)。
同调作用为
\[
 \Phi_*=I+A,\qquad
 A(x)=\sum_i a_i\langle x,[\gamma_i]\rangle[\gamma_i],
 \qquad A^2=0,\qquad\operatorname{rank}A=r.
\]
最后一个秩等式依赖所有系数同号：
若 \(C\) 的列为 \([\gamma_i]\)，则
\(A=CDC^{\mathsf T}J\)，\(D>0\)，
故其秩为曲线同调张成空间的维数；
曲面切割正合列又把该维数识别为 \(r\)。
对应分裂扩张的
\(b_1=2p_1+2q_1-r\)。
若 \(r\) 偶且非零，取 \(\Gamma\) 的连通二重覆盖，
沿曲面到对偶图的压缩映射拉回。
多扭转在该图上恒等，故相容提升；
新图的第一 Betti 数为 \(2r-1\)，于是得到奇数 \(b_1\) 的更深覆盖。
若 \(r\) 原本为奇数，无需最后一步。

紧 K\"ahler 流形及其有限覆盖的 \(b_1\) 均为偶数，故
\begin{equation}
 G\text{ 的任何有限指数子群都不是任何复维数的紧 K\"ahler 群}.
 \label{BUNTOP:not-virtually-kahler}
\end{equation}
注意量词是“每个覆盖有更深的奇数 \(b_1\) 覆盖”，
不是“所有覆盖本身均有奇数 \(b_1\)”。

\paragraph{非正截面曲率障碍是经典结果。}
Kapovich--Leeb 已在同一个单 twist 循环单值构造上证明，
\(G\) 无忠实、proper 且半单的 CAT(0) 等距作用，
且相应离散半单作用的存在性对有限指数变换保持。
这里 proper 是对群元素计数的适当性，
不把平凡作用误算成允许的离散作用。
因此所有有限覆盖都无 \(\sec\le0\) 度量。%
\footnote{\url{https://www.math.ucdavis.edu/~kapovich/EPR/KL_1996.pdf}，
Kapovich--Leeb，\emph{Actions of discrete groups on nonpositively curved spaces}，
Theorems 2.3、4.5。Theorem 4.5 的证明正是循环 Dehn twist 曲面丛。}
其单 twist 机制可直接看出。映射环面子群有关系
\[
 zaz^{-1}=a,\qquad zbz^{-1}=ba,\qquad
 zc_iz^{-1}=c_i,\quad zd_iz^{-1}=d_i,\qquad
 [a,b]\prod_{i\ge2}[c_i,d_i]=1.
\]
置 \(v=bab^{-1}\)，则 \(a,v,c_i,d_i\) 均与 \(z\) 交换。
在假设存在的半单 CAT(0) 作用中，
\(\operatorname{Min}(z)=Y\times\mathbb R\)
给中心化子的共同平移同态 \(\tau\)。
曲面关系给 \(\tau(a)=\tau(v)\)，
而 \(az\) 与 \(v^{-1}z\) 均与 \(z\) 共轭。
在两个相应 flat torus 上计算长度，得到
\[
 \tau(a)=-\frac{\ell(a)^2}{2\ell(z)}<0,\qquad
 \tau(v)=\frac{\ell(v)^2}{2\ell(z)}>0,
\]
矛盾。两个欧氏平面可以不同，关键是它们使用同一个 \(\tau\)。

更一般的循环单值族 \(E_\phi\) 中，
\[
 E_\phi\text{ 有 }\sec\le0\text{ 度量}
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad[\phi]\text{ 有限阶}.
\]
无限阶方向由 surface-by-surface CAT(0) 群的单值单射定理；
有限阶方向由 Nielsen realization 选不变双曲纤维度量，
其与基底双曲度量的乘积下降为 \(\operatorname{Ric}=-g\) 的度量。%
\footnote{\url{https://arxiv.org/pdf/2001.03694}，Llosa Isenrich--Py，Theorem 3。
有限阶的 realization 见
\url{https://annals.math.princeton.edu/1983/117-2/p02}，Kerckhoff 的原定理。}
无非正截面曲率、无复结构和非虚 K\"ahler，
均未构成一般负 Einstein 不存在性定理。


````

sec:bundle-global；当前是第8节，不继承旧稿的第7节编号。此为标注范围的原文摘录，不是全文或新证明。

````latex
\section{Near-Einstein constructions and global questions on a surface bundle}\label{sec:bundle-global}
\subsection{The fixed smooth object and the full slow-interpolation input}\label{sec:bundle-input}
Fix $F=\Sigma_p$ and $B=\Sigma_q$, with $p,q\ge2$.
Let $D$ be a positive Dehn twist about a nonseparating curve in $F$, with base point outside its support.
Given a primitive epimorphism $u:\pi_1(B)\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb Z$, define
\[
 E_D\longrightarrow B,\qquad
 \rho(\gamma)=[D]^{u(\gamma)},\qquad
 \pi_1(E_D)=\pi_1(F)\rtimes_{D_*^{u(\cdot)}}\pi_1(B).
\]
As the metric parameter varies, the mapping class of $D$, the data $p,q,u$, and the smooth total space all remain fixed.
This is stronger than obtaining small errors only on a sequence of varying topologies.

Fix a hyperbolic fiber collar
$h_0=du_0^2+a(u_0)^2ds^2$, $a=a_0\cosh u_0$.
For a smooth function $\zeta$ equal to $0,1$ near its ends, write
\[
 h_z=du_0^2+a^2(ds+z\zeta' du_0)^2,
 \quad h_1=D^*h_0,
 \quad K_{h_z}=-1,
 \quad dA_{h_z}=dA_{h_0}.
\]
The metrics match at the collar boundary. For nonintegral $z$, the local coordinate change need not be a global isotopy of the closed fiber.
Pinch a nonseparating base curve to length $\ell\to0$, and set
\[
 L=\tfrac12\operatorname{arsinh}(1/\sinh(\ell/2)),\quad
 b(t)=\ell\cosh t,\quad z(t)=\eta(t/L),\quad
 G_\ell=dt^2+b^2d\theta^2+h_{z(t)}.
\]
Here $\eta$ is constant near both ends. Use the hyperbolic product outside and glue by $D$ at the right end to obtain a smooth closed metric on the fixed $E_D$.
Set $j=\tanh u_0$, $k=\tanh t$, $Q=a\zeta'$, and $v=Qz'$.
In the orthonormal coframe $(dt,b\,d\theta,du_0,a(ds+z\zeta'du_0))$, the full defect is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bundleRicci}
 (\Ric_{G_\ell}+G_\ell)_{\rm ON}=
 \begin{pmatrix}
 -v^2/2&0&0&(v_{u_0}+2jv)/2\\
 0&0&0&0\\
 0&0&-v^2/2&-(v_t+kv)/2\\
 (v_{u_0}+2jv)/2&0&-(v_t+kv)/2&v^2/2
 \end{pmatrix}.
\end{equation}
The quadratic terms in the covariant derivative of the shape operator include frame rotation; ordinary differentiation of matrix entries alone is insufficient.
With $A_0=\|Q\|_\infty$, $A_1=\|Q'+2jQ\|_\infty$, and $M_i=\|\eta^{(i)}\|_\infty$, we obtain
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bundlebound}
 \begin{aligned}
 \delta_-(G_\ell)&\le\frac{A_0^2M_1^2}{2L^2}\\
 &\quad+\frac1{2L}\sqrt{A_1^2M_1^2+A_0^2(M_1+M_2/L)^2}\longrightarrow0.
 \end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Outside the error support the metric is an exact product. This is a global unweighted endomorphism bound, not an integrated error made small by volume collapse.
Moreover,
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \Vol(G_\ell)=V_0=16\pi^2(p-1)(q-1),\quad
 s=-4-v^2/2,\\
 \|\nabla^m\Rm\|_\infty\le C_m,\quad
 \|\nabla^m(\Ric+G_\ell)\|_\infty\le C_m/L.
 \end{gathered}
\]
The constants depend on the fixed surfaces, path, and derivative order; they are not uniform estimates for all pinched metrics.
The thick-part limit is $F\times\Sigma_{q-1,2}$, the interpolation region has volume $O(\sqrt\ell)$, the injectivity radius tends to zero, and the diameter diverges.
Since $K(e_0,e_3)=v^2/4$ is positive wherever the shear is nontrivial, stability theorems requiring everywhere negative sectional curvature do not apply.
The full frame calculation also appears in Chapter 6 of the \href{20260912.tex}{long paper}, at \path{BUN:slow}, \path{BUN:ricci-matrix}, and \path{BUN:slow-bound}.

The same chapter extends slow interpolation to monodromy factoring through a standard geometric free-group quotient,
$\rho(a_i)=1,\rho(b_i)=[\phi_i]$, allowing noncommuting $\phi_i$.
Moser area gauge and disjoint base collars preserve the construction mechanism.
These families still have zero signature. A surface bundle of nonzero signature satisfies
$\frac12\int|dF|^2\ge6\pi^2|\tau|$ in the Weil--Petersson normalization. Thus one cannot uniformly suppress every moduli velocity over a fixed-curvature base without changing the hypotheses.

\subsection{Higher-order corrections, TT obstructions, and degenerating inverses}
The following results concern the same fixed surface bundle.
Proofs are given at the indicated labels in Chapter 6 of the \href{20260912.tex}{long paper}; the background metric and perturbation scale are part of each statement.
\begin{longtable}{L{.23\textwidth}L{.65\textwidth}}
\toprule Label & Statement and scope\\\midrule\endhead
\path{BUN:double} & Simultaneous fiber pinching at $\varepsilon$ and base pinching at $\beta=\varepsilon^2$, with normalized $\operatorname{sech}^3u$ and $\operatorname{sech}t$ profiles, gives $\delta=2\varepsilon^2/\pi^4+O(\varepsilon^4)$. The endpoint Teichm\"uller distance also degenerates, so this does not violate a fixed-endpoint $L\delta$ bound.\\
\path{BUN:third} & Three radial tensor-correction equations, with Bianchi and tail checks, give after truncation a $C^k$ residual $O(\varepsilon^3)$ for each fixed $k$ on the same closed manifold. The correction generally destroys the original Riemannian submersion.\\
\path{BUN:global-obstruction} & A genuine global TT test tensor gives $c\varepsilon^3\le C\delta(G_\varepsilon+h)+C\varepsilon^2a+Ca^2$, where $a=\|h\|_{C^2}$. Corrections with $a=O(\varepsilon^2)$ have a third-order lower bound. This is an obstruction at a specified neighborhood scale, not a positive defect gap for arbitrary metrics.\\
\path{BUN:conformal} & For the third-order background $\widetilde G_\varepsilon$, the entire conformal class satisfies $\inf_{u>0}\delta(u^2\widetilde G_\varepsilon)=\Theta(\varepsilon^3)$. A retained product open set also excludes Einstein metrics of every sign in these conformal classes, but not in other classes.\\
\path{BUN:volume-expansion} & The third-order background has volume $V_0+\varepsilon^5/(2\pi^2)+O(\varepsilon^6\log(1/\varepsilon))$. Negative constant-scalar-curvature normalization preserves this leading correction. A conformal-class Yamabe value is not the manifold supremum.\\
\path{BUN:near-spectrum} & The original background Jacobian has negative index at least $6q-6$; after correction a specified two-dimensional Ritz compression changes sign. These are different backgrounds, and positivity of a finite compression does not prove invertibility of the full operator.\\
\path{BUN:inverse} & On a degenerating family of closed exact Einstein products, the $L^\infty$ inverse grows logarithmically even after removal of the entire true kernel, despite a uniform positive $L^2$ spectral gap. This tests uniform Newton arguments; it is not a Yau26 counterexample.\\\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

\subsection{Global topology and variational invariants}\label{sec:bundle-values}
The manifold $E_D$ is aspherical, with $\chi=4(p-1)(q-1)>0$, $\tau=0$, and $b_1=2p+2q-1$.
Every connected finite cover admits neither an integrable complex structure nor a metric of nonpositive sectional curvature. The complex-structure argument is at \path{BUNTOP:covers} in the \href{20260912.tex}{long paper}; the nonpositive-curvature obstruction is also proved in English at \path{lem:ED-no-npc} in the \href{20260916_construction.tex}{construction paper}.
These are not nonexistence theorems for general Einstein metrics.
The local Luttinger-surgery identification, covering monodromy, symplectic canonical classes, free-quotient-preserving operations, and the obstruction to moving the construction into an exceptional sphere are given at \path{BUNTOP:object}.

For a connected $d$-fold cover, put $V_*=dV_0$. The construction and monopole estimates give
\begin{align}
 Y&=-4\sqrt{V_*},&
 \inf\int s^2&=16V_*,&\inf\int|\Ric|^2&=4V_*,\notag\\
 \inf_{s\ge-4}\Vol&=\inf_{\Ric\ge-g}\Vol=V_*,&
 \inf\int|\Rm_{\rm full}|^2&=8V_* .\label{eq:bundlefunctionals}
\end{align}
The infima range over all smooth metrics on this fixed cover, while $Y$ is the supremum of Yamabe constants over all conformal classes.
For each orientation, the actual canonical monopole class has square $2\chi(\widehat E)=V_*/(2\pi^2)$.
LeBrun's scalar and Ricci estimates give $\int s^2\ge16V_*$ and $\int|\Ric|^2\ge4V_*$.
The lifted explicit metrics have volume $V_*$ and $\Ric+g\to0$ uniformly, giving the matching limiting upper bounds.
The two-sided volume and Yamabe estimates and their equality cases appear at \path{BUNTOP:global-values} in the \href{20260912.tex}{long paper} and in \cite[Proposition 3.2 and Theorem 5.3]{LeBrun2006}.
Equality in these Yamabe, scalar, Ricci, and volume extrema forces a K\"ahler structure, so none is attained.

Full curvature energy has a different metricwise identity. By \eqref{eq:CGB} and our full-tensor convention,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bundle-full-energy}
 \int_{\widehat E}|\Rm_{\rm full}|^2\dd
 =32\pi^2\chi(\widehat E)+4\int_{\widehat E}|E|^2\dd
 =8V_*+4\int_{\widehat E}|E|^2\dd .
\end{equation}
The explicit inputs make the second term tend to zero, proving that the infimum is $8V_*$.
Attainment is equivalent to $E=0$; the contracted Bianchi identity then makes the Einstein factor constant:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bundle-attainment-equivalence}
 \exists g:\int|\Rm_{\rm full}|^2\dd=8V_*
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \widehat E\text{ carries an Einstein metric}.
\end{equation}
This attainment problem remains open. Nonattainment for the other functionals does not transfer to it, and an arbitrary Einstein critical point need not realize the scalar, Ricci, or volume infimum.
The same input sequence saturates the convex-hull and common-polarization numerical bounds for all monopole classes in both orientations. Adding more basic classes therefore does not automatically produce a strict numerical gap.

A positive-square monopole class excludes nonnegative constants for a hypothetical exact Einstein metric.
After normalizing its negative constant by $\Ric=-g$, its volume satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bundle-einstein-window}
 V_*<\Vol(g)<3V_*.
\end{equation}
The strict lower bound follows from nonattainment of minimal Ricci volume.
The upper bound follows from $2V_*=\int|W|^2+\tfrac23\Vol(g)$; equality would give $W=0$, hence constant sectional curvature $-1/3$, contradicting the nonpositive-curvature obstruction.
An Einstein metric with volume in this open interval would attain the infimum in \eqref{eq:bundle-full-energy} but none of the other infima in \eqref{eq:bundlefunctionals}; there is no contradiction.
It must also have a positive-sectional-curvature plane, and each Weyl half must have three distinct eigenvalues on a common open dense set.
Proposition \path{BUNTOP:Einstein-necessary} further gives volume constancy along connected normalized exact Einstein paths. This excludes a connected exact branch approaching $V_*$, not other volumes, other branches, or discrete exact sequences.
Indeed, for a $C^1$ path $g(a)$ on a connected parameter interval with $\Ric_{g(a)}=-g(a)$, set $h=\partial_ag$ and $V(a)=\Vol(g(a))$. The first variation of total scalar curvature gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bundle-exact-path-volume}
 -4V'=\frac d{da}\int s\,dV
 =\int\left(-\langle\Ric,h\rangle+\frac s2\tr h\right)dV
 =-\int\tr h\,dV=-2V',
 \qquad V'=0.
\end{equation}
Thus inputs of volume tending to $V_*$ cannot be corrected, over a whole small parameter interval, to a $C^1$ exact path by relative $C^0$ perturbations tending to zero.
This does not exclude remote solutions or disconnected exact sequences.
Fixing a nonzero Einstein constant is essential to volume constancy.

\paragraph{An additional exact-sequence hypothesis at the lower endpoint.}\label{par:bundle-endpoint-scope}
If one \emph{additionally assumes} an exact Einstein sequence whose volumes tend to $V_*$, then \path{BUNTOP:endpoint} and \path{BUNTOP:reducible-core} give bounded curvature, thick points, and a noncompact finite-volume $\mathbb H^2\times\mathbb H^2$ limit.
They exclude the irreducible-lattice branch and give integral periods, fundamental-group injectivity for closed factors, and genus-divisibility conditions for reducible cores.
Compatible product cores do exist within the candidate topology. It is not proved that the limit captures all volume or that the entire core is $\pi_1$-injective.
These are conditional degeneration conclusions for a \emph{hypothetical exact sequence}, not descriptions of the already constructed approximate sequence.

\subsection{Submersion structure, harmonic representatives, and source terms}\label{sec:bundle-structure-bridge}

Einstein rigidity under an adapted fibration, horizontal integrability, or basic mean curvature restricts \emph{adapted metrics}, not arbitrary Einstein metrics. Surface-bundle topology does not require an unknown Einstein metric to respect the original fibration.
Precisely, \path{SUB:integrable} assumes a closed Riemannian submersion with closed, connected, oriented higher-genus fibers and integrable horizontal distribution.
For a closed four-dimensional submersion with negative Einstein total metric, \path{SUB:basic} assumes that the unnormalized mean-curvature one-form is pulled back from the base.
The resulting product after a finite cover contradicts the infinite Dehn-twist monodromy.
Normalizing fiber volume changes mean curvature but generally destroys the original Einstein equation; the two properties cannot be combined across different metrics.

There is no need initially to assume that the projection is a Riemannian submersion.
Fix an arbitrary hypothetical metric with $\Ric_G=-cG$, $c>0$.
For each marked hyperbolic target $(B,h)$, $K_h=-1$, the epimorphism on fundamental groups ensures a unique harmonic representative $f_h\simeq\pi$.
Its rank-two set is open and dense, but it may still have critical points.
Let $s_1,s_2$ be the two target singular values of $df_h$, and put
$t=|df_h|^2=s_1^2+s_2^2$, $J=|f_h^*\omega_h|=s_1s_2$.
Each of the two ordered target-curvature contributions equals $J^2$. The Bochner identity and integration on the closed source give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bundle-harmonic-defect}
 \frac12\Delta_Gt=|\nabla df_h|^2-ct+2J^2,\qquad
 1-\mathcal R_{\rm harm}
 =\frac{\int|\nabla df_h|^2}{c\int|df_h|^2},\qquad
 \mathcal R_{\rm harm}:=\frac{2\int J^2}{c\int|df_h|^2}.
\end{equation}
If $\mathcal R_{\rm harm}=1$, then $df_h$ is parallel. Its kernel and orthogonal complement are parallel, and the source is locally a product of two surfaces of curvature $-c$.
The proper submersion and fundamental-group epimorphism give connected closed fibers; parallel transport takes values in the finite isometry group of the fiber, contradicting the original infinite outer monodromy.
Every hypothetical Einstein metric on this candidate therefore satisfies $0<\mathcal R_{\rm harm}<1$.
The strict inequality is not a uniform defect bound over all source metrics.
Horizontal conformality additionally requires
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bundle-harmonic-anisotropy}
 t^2-4J^2=(s_1^2-s_2^2)^2=0,
\end{equation}
which follows neither from harmonicity nor from \eqref{eq:bundle-harmonic-defect}.

The missing condition on the area form can also be identified exactly.
Set $\alpha=f_h^*\omega_h$ and $B_f=\nabla df_h$. Then $d\alpha=0$, while harmonicity gives only
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bundle-area-source}
 (\delta_G\alpha)(Y)
 =-\sum_i\omega_h\bigl(df_h(e_i),B_f(e_i,Y)\bigr),
\end{equation}
whose right-hand side is generally nonzero.
For a class $u_B$ of integral one, put
$\beta=\mathsf H_G(\pi^*u_B)$ and $\xi=\mathsf H_Ge(\ker d\pi)$, where $\mathsf H_G$ denotes the Hodge representative. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bundle-euler-blindness}
 \alpha=\operatorname{Area}(B,h)\beta+d\nu,\qquad
 \int\alpha\wedge\xi=\operatorname{Area}(B,h)\chi(F),\qquad
 \delta_G\alpha=\delta_Gd\nu.
\end{equation}
The Euler pairing eliminates precisely the exact error that still needs to be controlled.
The vertical bundle here is that of the original smooth fibration; it cannot be replaced by $\ker df_h$ at critical points.
Complete proofs and counterexamples appear in the long paper at \path{SUB:harmonic-bochner}, \path{SUB:pullback-area-derivative}, and \path{SUB:euler-hodge}.

An energy-minimizing harmonic map to a nonpositively curved surface is not automatically a harmonic morphism or horizontally conformal.
A product of hyperbolic surfaces mapping to a differently marked hyperbolic surface already gives an Einstein source without the required conformality.
Optimizing the target Teichm\"uller structure gives an integrated TT stationarity condition, not pointwise vanishing of a Hopf-type defect.
For the unique optimal marked target of a fixed source, specifically,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bundle-optimal-target-condition}
 \frac12\int_{E_D}\tr_G f_h^*k\,dV_G=0
 \qquad\text{for every target TT tensor }k.
\end{equation}
This condition first integrates over fibers and then tests a finite-dimensional target space; it is not pointwise vanishing in \eqref{eq:bundle-harmonic-anisotropy}.
The counterexample at \path{SUB:optimal-nonconformal} has a genuinely unique optimal target, but its nonzero-parameter sources are only near-Einstein and have product topology. It is not an exact counterexample on $E_D$.
A global obstruction would require a new coercive quantity arising from the specific infinite Dehn-twist monodromy, spectral degeneration, or conformality defect.

Possible decisive directions are to show that every Einstein metric induces a controlled harmonic-map structure, to derive from monodromy an integral identity incompatible with the Einstein equation, or to construct an Einstein metric on $E_D$ by a genuinely non-K\"ahler method.
Other candidates may admit different complex structures, but this option is already excluded for $E_D$.
Each direction requires its full hypotheses; absence of a complex structure is not absence of an Einstein metric.

Related identities and counterexamples appear in Chapters 7--10 of the \href{20260912.tex}{long paper}:
\begin{itemize}
\item Chapter 7, \path{WYL:source}: the sourced Weyl-eigenline relations
$q=\tfrac32\ell_1-\eta H_1$ and
$(9+4\eta^2)D-4\eta^2N=4|q|^2+8\eta\langle q,H_1\rangle$.
In slow interpolation, the source is $q=O(L^{-1})$, whereas the relative quantity needed by the source-free argument is only $O(L^{-2})$.
Small divergence is not harmonic Weyl curvature. Near-closedness also does not remove the harmonic compression kernel.
\item Chapter 8, \path{SUB:blocks}: the full submersion Ricci equations with twist, nonbasic mean curvature, and mixed sources.
The labels \path{SUB:integrable} and \path{SUB:basic} give product rigidity under horizontal integrability or basic mean curvature.
The calculation at \path{SUB:quartic} retains the fourth moments and covariance generated by variation of measure, while \path{SUB:negative-covariance} constructs an actual local Einstein model with negative covariance.
Closed extension preserving the specified fiber geometry is separately restricted by \path{SUB:extension-gap}.
\item Chapter 9, \path{SUB:harmonic-bochner}: under any hypothetical $\Ric=-cG$, the harmonic representative satisfies
$\int|\nabla df|^2+2\int J^2=c\int|df|^2$; only the zero-defect equality gives a product.
Closedness of the area form is not coclosedness; Hodge projection does not preserve pointwise decomposability, and the Euler pairing does not detect the exact error.
\item \path{SUB:optimal-target}: a fixed source has a unique optimal marked hyperbolic target, but the first variation tests only the target TT space.
The closed near-Einstein family at \path{SUB:optimal-nonconformal} still has a non-horizontally-conformal map to its unique optimal target.
\item Chapter 10, \path{GLB:WPbalance}: the Weil--Petersson length direction of cyclic Dehn-twist monodromy obstructs the unforced effective equation.
With a source, however, one must have $\int w\,d\ell(R)=-\int w\operatorname{Hess}\ell(du,du)$.
The full Einstein equation has not been shown to give relative source control excluding this cancellation.
\end{itemize}
These examples distinguish a small source, integrated stationarity, pointwise conformality, and an exact submersion.


````

# Verification record

23 September 2026. Scope: the three manuscripts listed in
`START_LINE1_COMPOSITION_20260923.md`.

## Reproduction

`verify_line1_composition.py` ran nine check programs successfully.
The three new programs contain 22, 23 and 19 exact checks, respectively;
the other six exercise the inherited algebra used by the proofs.
Commands, return codes, output and manuscript hashes are preserved in
`line1_composition_evidence/`. Five local manuscript links were checked.
These are finite algebra and artifact checks, not automated verification
of the global PDE or gauge-theoretic existence arguments.

The functional, actual-form and integral-rigidity manuscripts compiled
with XeLaTeX to 8, 5 and 21 pages. Their final logs contain no undefined
references, missing-character reports, overfull boxes or LaTeX errors.
The long manuscript retains harmless font-size substitution and underfull
box warnings. Compiler logs are retained alongside the check outputs.

## Independent mathematical reading

The separate `line1_direction_review` agent read the functional proof
and then its added harmonic-alignment section; its findings and separate
symbolic checks are recorded in `line1_functional_pencil_review.md`.

For the standalone integral-rigidity paper, `r21_analytic` independently
read the weak Bianchi self-testing argument in Section 6, including its
signs, normalization and testing regularity. It found no substantive
issue. `line1_direction_review` read the abstract, Section 1 and Sections
7–11 against the polarization closure and chamber sources. It found no
mathematical discrepancy in those sections, conditional on the analytic
core and cited classical gauge inputs. The reported notation and layout
errors were corrected before the final compilation. The main agent also
read the analytic proof and the remaining manuscript sections.

These are internal, bounded reviews, not external peer review or a proof
of publication priority. The new functional statement continues to use
the inherited bounded-form compatibility and bundle Hessian arguments;
their sources remain in the package. The standalone main paper includes
the analytic derivation instead of citing a round number as its proof.

## Scope of the results

The new functional theorem gives quantitative integral non-saturation
under its stated actual-class assumptions. The endpoint application
strictly removes the earlier pointwise scalar-band assumption, with an
actual conformal family witnessing the larger domain. Neither result
establishes a new smooth topological family with no Einstein metric.
The endpoint conformal example concerns integral, not pointwise, Ricci
error. No complete Yau26 sign branch is claimed.

The existing Skill conventions and proof lineage were retained; this
research package does not overwrite the installed v08/v09 Skills or any
running Session.

# Independent proof review

23 September 2026. Reviewer: the separate `line1_direction_review`
agent. The review concerns the present functional manuscript, not all
historical results in the repository.

## Main argument

The reviewer read the original 447-line manuscript (SHA-256
`120064f7fa83903e03e816207a319db28a2e3d5af0e014bc383960df23f7c2df`)
and found no mathematical error in its new argument, conditional on the
inherited bounded-form compatibility theorem and bundle Hessian estimate.
The checks covered the signed deficit decomposition, cutoff costs,
actual versus ideal curvature moments, simultaneous amplitude and Weyl
direction fitting, second-order budget, square-cost reduction, large
defect case, and endpoint normalizations.

Eight independent SymPy identities were checked without calling the
author's checker: the functional square split, form normalization,
arbitrary-direction Weyl identity, beta-two and beta-eight identities,
KE endpoint order, classical lower-parameter bound, and the hypothetical
topological ratio. All residuals were zero. These are algebra checks,
not verification of gauge solvability.

The review requested two clarifications, now included: absorption of
the bounded beta-fourth factor in the main estimate, and compatibility
with the chosen Spin-c structure in the cscK equality statement.

## Added harmonic-alignment theorem

The reviewer separately read the subsequently added Section 7 (manuscript
SHA-256 `13f0324dab4999ffba998cd60e8a1d8c152b97aae6f40218aca599230c4201ae`) and
checked its cross-references and equations 1–29. The harmonic coefficient
`-3*pi*beta/(beta+1)`, curvature remainder `4*S/9`, canonical alignment
constant 49, derivative estimate `13*kappa + 4*sqrt(2*kappa)`, exterior
derivative bound `13*kappa/2`, and spectral-tail bound all checked out.
The intermediate weight estimate is conservative. The proof does not
assume that the harmonic representative has a maximum bound.

The bounded-form derivative estimate initially applies at small
calibration error; the remaining compact defect range is correctly
handled by the integrated Hodge identity. The second-order square cost
does not enter the stronger endpoint first-derivative estimate.

## Literature and scope

Exact Einstein saturation implying KE is already LeBrun's Corollary 2.5.
The new statement is quantitative functional non-saturation under
integral Ricci error and an explicit square budget. The classical
scalar and scalar–Weyl constraints already give the sharper elementary
bound for beta at most two. Their relaxed moment constraints do not
eliminate the formal beta-greater-than-two calibrations.

This comparison is not an exhaustive novelty audit. The formal
calibration data used to compare beta-eight with other parameters do
not assert existence of metrics realizing those data. No new
topological Einstein obstruction follows without additional application
input.

# Two-chirality application: verification and provenance

23 September 2026. Worktree:

Skill, research Session, runtime, service, or historical proof.

## Mathematical dependency

The new theorem uses the existing `line1_energy_envelope_gap.md`, which
in turn uses `line1_functional_pencil.md`. Those analytical dependencies
are not independently recertified by this algebraic transfer.

The new proof supplies the previously missing upper bound on *every*
Einstein candidate, from the opposite-chirality Chern--Weil formula.
No opposite-orientation monopole class is assumed. Combined with the
parameter-dependent lower envelope this gives an all-metric relative
Einstein-defect gap controlled by both Euler/signature combinations.

The floor construction produces actual smooth manifolds using compact
complex-hyperbolic covers, exceptional connected sums, and handle
additions. Gauge nonvanishing, loop insertions, class signs, and positive
weights use the already written polarized closure, not arithmetic alone.
Ricci-flatness is separately excluded by the positive scalar monopole
budget. The pointwise corollary covers both normalized target signs.

  insertions; Corollary 2.5: classical exact equality forces KE.

  residual-finiteness input; the unbounded-index deduction is given in

The examples pass the explicitly compared classical inequalities and
the prior package's stated one-third stability constant. This is a

The new note proves constant-sign alignment of actual positive Chern periods
under the endpoint deficit and a quantitative scalar Poincare bound. Its
main cup consequence is a bound on the second largest eigenvalue of the
normalized intersection matrix. Its matrix decomposition retains the
uncontrolled negative Gram matrix. It assumes neither common positive
class square nor a lower bound by `2 chi + 3 tau`.

  alignment, Weyl fit and first derivative estimates.

  theorem, and Round 2's measurable alignment and Walsh argument.

  These check finite tensor/phase algebra, constants, normalization,
  polarization and a positive/negative Gram model. They do not prove the
  endpoint gauge/PDE input or replace the analytic proof.

  inequalities at zeros, Poincare use, constants, finite-sign subsequence,
  Hodge normalization, min--max, pair obstruction, spectral alternative,
  and the exact common-functional refinement. No blocking mathematical

  form" with "matrix form", was applied; the displayed matrix bounds are

# 本正文版的来源、范围和未选历史稿

以下每项至少提供一份目录登记原稿全文，不代表该结果族的所有历史版本都已纳入。
manuscript-theorem只表示有成文论证，不等于独立认证。程序与原始数据保留本地，复算时另取。

## I01 — Exact monopole–Euler uniform Einstein gap

状态：`manuscript-theorem`。

scope: Exact Einstein four-manifolds with the stated metricwise monopole projection budget MP(A), b_+ > 1 and chi <= L A.

statement: T/A >= 2/3 + eta(L); the blowup application is uniform across the specified general-type family.

boundary: This local-development proof is not the later intrinsic integral proof and does not alone establish approximate-input regularity.

- 全文：`01_主线入口与核心障碍.md` 原稿 10，`mathematics/current/skill/research/20260916_uniform_gap.tex`。
- 按需原稿：`mathematics/current/skill/research/20260916_rigidity.tex`。早期综合长稿另含局部、流与其他方法；专门精确间隙稿和后来的独立积分主稿已提供，不声称其余方法全被替代。

## I02 — Intrinsic integral monopole calibration and all-sign defect gap

状态：`manuscript-theorem`。

scope: Actual conformally solvable monopole input, positive self-dual projection budget, and the stated class-square condition; arbitrary smooth metrics.

statement: A uniform relative integral Einstein gap holds in the near-critical topology band without Sobolev, diameter, noncollapse or full-curvature bounds; arbitrary classes retain their negative-square cost.

boundary: A formal cohomology vector is insufficient. This is not a topology-free integral theorem.

- 全文：`01_主线入口与核心障碍.md` 原稿 4，`mathematics/current/line1_integral_rigidity_paper.md`。
- 按需原稿：`mathematics/current/round4_rigidity.md`。原积分校准稿；独立主稿已纳入分析证明，原文件留档按需。
- 按需原稿：`mathematics/current/round4_all_sign_gap.md`。早期全符号推导；当前主稿和应用闭合全文已提供。
- 按需原稿：`mathematics/current/round5_rigidity.md`。原输入与推广记录；当前独立主稿提供实际输入及证明，原版本留档按需。

## I03 — Polarized, b_+=1 and handle-addition closure

状态：`manuscript-theorem`。

scope: Positive-budget SW bases with the specified chamber/dimension/insertion conditions, including minimal general-type surfaces and the stated positive-canonical symplectic b_+=1 bases.

statement: Metricwise polarization selects one actual class valid for all positive conformal weights. The quantitative classical blowup/handle applications include b_+=1; positive scalar monopole energy separately excludes Ricci-flat metrics.

boundary: This strengthens the specified LeBrun Einstein-obstruction chain, not every result of his 2001 paper.

- 按需原稿：`mathematics/current/round13_chamber.md`。早期极化接口；闭合稿、独立主稿及更晚 chamber 文本已提供。

- 全文：`01_主线入口与核心障碍.md` 原稿 6，`mathematics/current/round21_chambers.md`。
- 按需原稿：`mathematics/current/round21_comparison.md`。历史定位补充，当前指导与主稿负责本次阅读入口；不是删除历史。

## I04 — Twisted monopole integral stability

状态：`manuscript-theorem`。

scope: Actual Pin^-(2) monopole classes and invariant positive weights on the associated isometric double cover, with the listed projection and square budgets.

statement: The integral mechanism gives a uniform critical-family gap even when ordinary SW and ordinary stable-cohomotopy invariants downstairs vanish.

boundary: Solvability is required only for lifted metrics and necessary deck-invariant weights, not claimed for all metrics on the cover.

- 全文：`01_主线入口与核心障碍.md` 原稿 8，`mathematics/current/round14_twisted_rigidity.md`。
- 全文：`01_主线入口与核心障碍.md` 原稿 7，`mathematics/current/round14_rigidity.md`。

## I05 — Bounded two-form differential compatibility

状态：`manuscript-theorem`。

scope: A bounded smooth self-dual two-form, bounded constant model amplitude, the specified Ricci/Weyl/amplitude defect d, and Hodge-Laplacian budget B.

statement: The product of defect and derivative budget controls distance to the allowed amplitudes 0 and -sigma/2; the negative forbidden amplitude 1/8 has a uniform incompatibility gap.

boundary: The form must actually be supplied in applications. The rank-one Weyl model is not a classification of arbitrary Einstein curvature.

- 全文：`02_参数泛函与双手性拓扑间隙.md` 原稿 1，`mathematics/current/round21_analytic.md`。

## I06 — Actual form supply aligned with harmonic Chern data

状态：`manuscript-theorem`。

scope: Actual conformal monopole solvability with small negative-Einstein Ricci defect plus beta=2 scalar–Weyl deficit; a positive self-dual projection.

statement: One bounded smooth weighted-monopole form simultaneously has harmonic alignment, amplitude/Weyl approximation, first-order control and a joint Hodge-Laplacian budget. Only the second-order estimate retains negative-class-square cost.

boundary: H is the self-dual harmonic representative, not the whole Chern class; average almost-parallelism does not prove exact global Kähler geometry.

## I07 — Parameterized scalar–Weyl non-saturation

状态：`manuscript-theorem`。

scope: Actual conformally solvable class with projection p>0; finite beta interval [2,L], arbitrary scalar curvature and the displayed negative-square cost.

statement: (1+b_a)(r+D_beta/V) >= c_L(beta-2)^2; for special square control the cost is absorbed. Beta=2 is the actual cscK/negative-KE endpoint and beta=8 recovers the critical topological combination.

boundary: Completing the classical square only proves D_beta >= 0; the new analytical content is uniform non-saturation with Ricci error.

- 全文：`02_参数泛函与双手性拓扑间隙.md` 原稿 2，`mathematics/current/line1_functional_pencil.md`。

## I08 — Energy envelope and two-chirality topology gap

状态：`manuscript-theorem`。

scope: Each metric has a selected actual conformal class with (a^+)^2 >= A and a^2 >= T_+; h=1-T_+/A in (0,1/3), U=3 min(T_+,T_-)/A.

statement: E+(U-F(h))_+ >= c h^2, F(h)=(4-sqrt(1-3h))^2/9. Realized mixed ball-quotient examples approach h=19/300 and the classical feasibility boundary.

boundary: Only one chirality needs the actual gauge supplier. Internal expansion beyond the old inequality is not a worldwide novelty certificate.

- 全文：`02_参数泛函与双手性拓扑间隙.md` 原稿 3，`mathematics/current/line1_energy_envelope_gap.md`。

## I09 — Compactified functional pencil and upper energy wall

状态：`manuscript-theorem`。

scope: Same actual inputs; all finite beta>=2 with explicit decay; upper-energy conclusion assumes 1/4<h<1/3.

statement: (1+b_a)(r+D_beta/V)>=c(beta-2)^2/beta^4. An all-sign transfer gives E+(G(h)-e)_+ >= c Theta(h), quantitatively shrinking the upper classical Einstein energy boundary as well as the lower one.

boundary: The endpoint decay is required and not asserted optimal. The classical bare roots F and G are not new.

- 全文：`02_参数泛函与双手性拓扑间隙.md` 原稿 5，`mathematics/current/line1_compactified_pencil.md`。

statement: A realized infinite family with ordinary SW vanishing and no almost-complex structure in either orientation has an all-metric integral gap uniformly bounded away from zero, and hence both pointwise signwise gaps.

boundary: Refined nonvanishing is classical; this is not a new construction of near-Einstein inputs or a Yau26 counterexample.

statement: No nonflat smooth ASD Ricci-flat ALE compact core embeds. The negative-definite case and rational-ball quotient case are both treated, yielding full-curvature control from one Weyl-half bound.

boundary: No spin assumption is used. This is not an arbitrary bounded-Ricci compactness theorem.

## I19 — Compact-regime exact realization from pointwise Ricci input

状态：`manuscript-theorem`。

scope: Closed connected smooth oriented aspherical four-manifolds with diameter<=D, volume>=v, and one Weyl half bounded in Lq by Lambda, q>2.

statement: There is epsilon(D,v,Lambda,q)>0 such that pointwise negative Ricci defect below epsilon implies a smooth negative Einstein metric on the same smooth manifold. Harmonic compactness is proved for the corresponding bounded-Ricci class.

boundary: The diameter, noncollapse and supercritical one-sided Weyl data are genuine restrictions. The threshold is not dimension-only, and this manuscript does not settle a full Yau26 sign branch.

- 全文：`05_紧致范围精确实现.md` 原稿 1，`continuation/20260923_compact_realization.md`。

## I20 — ALE core exclusion beyond aspherical topology

状态：`manuscript-theorem`。

scope: Closed oriented four-manifolds with torsion-free pi1 and pi2=0, including connected sums and S1xS3 additions.

statement: Half-flat nonflat Ricci-flat ALE cores cannot embed. An arbitrary finite-pi1 core with rational-homology-sphere boundary that embeds must be a rational homology ball.

boundary: Does not assert contractibility or exclude hypothetical mixed-chirality rational-ball ALE cores.

- 全文：`05_紧致范围精确实现.md` 原稿 2，`continuation/20260923_topological_core_scope.md`。

## I21 — Critical variable-chirality compactness and paired concentration

状态：`manuscript-theorem`。

scope: I20 topology, bounded two-sided Ricci and diameter, positive volume lower bound, and a fixed-radius local threshold on the minimum of the two chiral L2 energies.

statement: The controlled chirality may vary by ball. The threshold gives harmonic compactness and small-pointwise-defect same-smooth-type realization; degeneration otherwise forces definite mass in both Weyl halves at the same location.

boundary: This is a sufficient critical threshold, not a sharp constant or a general classification of Ricci-flat ALE spaces.

- 全文：`05_紧致范围精确实现.md` 原稿 3，`continuation/20260923_critical_chiral_threshold.md`。

## I22 — Actual gauge saturation gives same-type negative KE realization

状态：`manuscript-theorem`。

scope: I20 topology, diam<=D, an actual conformally solvable Spin-c class with self-dual projection p>=A0>0, and small delta_minus plus D2/V.

statement: A fixed epsilon(D,A0)>0 forces a compatible negative Kahler-Einstein metric on the same oriented smooth manifold, without passing to a cover or assuming external Weyl bounds or negative-square cost. Positive integral class square permits A0=1.

boundary: Does not claim that the input is Kahler or that pointwise Ricci smallness forces saturation. Diameter cannot be omitted: see X08.

## I23 — Averaged spectral compactness and exact allowed-mode rigidity

状态：`manuscript-theorem`。

scope: I20 topology, exact Ric=-g, bounded diameter and positive volume lower bound, small averaged upper-eigenvalue Weyl splitting.

statement: Small splitting prevents ALE bubbles without pointwise Weyl bounds. On products of hyperbolic surfaces it forces the exact metric to be locally H2xH2 uniformly over genera; on the specified nonzero-shear types it yields a diameter-only splitting gap without relative pinching.

boundary: Exact-input theorem; arbitrary KE local mode selection keeps unobstructedness. It neither classifies all Einstein metrics on products nor excludes all outputs on shear manifolds.

statement: A small pointwise Ricci defect implies an exact same-smooth-type Einstein metric of the prescribed sign. Positive Ricci gives the diameter bound by Myers. On the negative side actual positive monopole projection supplies the volume lower bound without saturation.

boundary: This is a composition with a credited existing no-bubble theorem, not a new classification of all ALE spaces. Diameter/volume conditions still restrict the original problem.

statement: The same smooth manifold carries a negative Einstein metric with universal cover H2(-1) x H2(-1), and global parallel complex structures in both orientations. Both Weyl halves disappear in any harmonic-radius blowup; no ALE or topological exclusion is used.

statement: The local radius and nonlinear repair constants have no diameter or total-volume dependence. The repaired metric is Einstein on the original smooth manifold, without imposing an input symmetry or complex structure.

boundary: The local regularity theorem does not supply the global spectral gap. Product Teichmuller kernels and small averaged residuals are not silently discarded.
