statement: Normalized Weyl spectral error descends without degree loss. Compactness of the base gives exact locally bidisk or complex-hyperbolic geometry on its original smooth type, without assuming class, form, or Einstein-metric descent.

boundary: Output need not be globally Kahler. The explicit non-almost-complex product quotient is a classical calibration, not a new Einstein construction. Diameter and saturation restrictions remain.

## I29 — Growth-coercivity Einstein repair with uniformly local residual

状态：`manuscript-theorem`。

statement: A uniformly local Holder inverse and small pointwise Ricci error yield exact same-manifold negative Einstein repair, independent of diameter and total volume. Cyclic hyperbolic covers admit distributed perturbations whose total L2 residual diverges while the repair applies.

boundary: Positive coercivity is stronger than an absolute spectral gap. No kernels are deleted; uncontrolled growth and product Teichmuller channels remain outside. Examples prove a uniform repair neighbourhood, not new Einstein topology.

状态：`manuscript-theorem`。

scope: Oriented surface bundles of fiber and base genus at least two, one uniformly thick conformal fiber, and small actual accumulated trace-free deformation around generators. Ricci corollary assumes a minimal Riemannian submersion.

statement: Small cycle deformation forces one finite mapping-class image, then exact bidisk KE on the same smooth manifold. With hyperbolic base, 2 diam(B) sqrt(delta_-) < epsilon(f,iota) suffices; no total-space diameter or intrinsic fiber-curvature condition. Actual Dehn-twist sequences independently separate long-cycle and thin-fiber failures.

## I33 — Full integrable-kernel repair and sharp cyclic scale loss

状态：`manuscript-theorem`。

scope: Products B_m x F of fixed hyperbolic surfaces with a degree-m cyclic cover in one factor; all tensor perturbations in harmonic gauge in a c m^-2 uniformly local C3alpha neighborhood.

statement: The entire Teichmuller kernel integrates to an actual Einstein family with uniformly controlled projections and all reduced equations zero. Transverse C1alpha-to-C3alpha inverse and nonlinear repair cost C m^2. Actual metrics have residual O(m^-4) and distance at least c m^-2 from all normalized Einstein metrics and pullbacks.

boundary: The topology already admits exact Einstein metrics. This quantifies repair and its nonuniformity; it is not an unrestricted low-mode theorem or Einstein nonexistence.

scope: Block metrics gamma+h_v(x) from a unit L2-speed horizontal path of actual hyperbolic fiber metrics; variable base curvature is retained. Cyclic tests use fixed-amplitude parameters on genuine degree-m covers.

statement: Every Ricci block and normal acceleration enters an exact nonlinear energy identity; the slow equation is Delta v/2. Actual canonical classes give q_plus=q_minus=9 Dir(v)/(4V)+9r/2 on hyperbolic base, with exact variable-curvature extension. Fixed-amplitude cyclic metrics have delta Theta(m^-2), relative defect Theta(m^-4), total Ricci-error energy Theta(m^-3), and fixed separation from all fiber-preserving product outputs.

## I37 — Actual harmonic-zero scale cost and defect-to-core matching

状态：`manuscript-theorem`。

scope: Actual conformal monopole near-saturation supplies H; coordinate charts have fixed rescaled C1alpha bounds. Marked filling application retains I31 orbifold assumptions and I34 A1/Betti or rational-ball topology.

statement: Actual harmonic amplitude error is at most 818(r+q)V. A zero in a controlled rho-ball costs at least c rho^4, so (r+q)V < c rho_core^4 implies whole-core nonvanishing, marked Milnor matching and, under separate global QG smoothing, negative KE on the same smooth manifold. Actual-class, period-preserving harmonic zero circles test why average form data alone are insufficient.

boundary: Raw near-Einstein input is not shown to force the core-scale ratio. The zero-circle example has large pointwise Ricci error and is not a Yau26 counterexample.

- 全文：`07_全局转移慢模态与填充.md` 原稿 8，`continuation/20260924_harmonic_zero_scale.md`。
